\mathbb{S}^{\mu_{I}(\alpha)}_{A,M}:=\mathbb{S}^{\alpha^{1}}_{A}\otimes_{R}\mathbb{S}^{\alpha^{2}}_{A}\otimes_{R}\cdots\otimes_{R}\mathbb{S}^{\alpha^{\ell-|I|}_{\leqslant g-1}}_{A,M_{\geqslant\alpha^{\ell-|I|}_{g}+s}}.

As a quick example let
\alpha=(2,1)\cdot(3,4)\cdot(4,3,3)
be a
3
-partitioned composition and
I=\{1,3\}\subset[3]
. Then for any Koszul algebra
A
and left
A
-module
M
of initial degree
s
, there is an equality

\mathbb{S}^{\mu_{I}(\alpha)}_{A,M}:=\mathbb{S}^{(2,4,4)}_{A}\otimes_{R}\mathbb{S}^{(4,3)}_{A,M_{\geqslant 3+s}}.

Remark 4.39
.

The slight technicality in defining
\mathbb{S}^{\mu_{I}(\alpha)}_{A,M}
when taking account of a module input stems from the fact that
M
has its own initial degree, in which case we should be defining the corresponding ribbons with respect to the composition
\alpha\cdot(s)
.

The following observation is an immediate consequence of the identification of the ribbon Schur functors with the modules of Definition
3.11
, noted in Observation
4.42
.

Observation 4.40
.

Let
A
be a Koszul algebra and
M
any left
A
-module. Given an
\ell
-partitioned composition
\alpha
and subsets
I\subset J\subset[\ell]
, there is a canonical morphism of
R
-modules

\mathbb{S}^{\mu_{I}(\alpha)}_{A}\to\mathbb{S}^{\mu_{J}(\alpha)}_{A}.

The following observation is a direct consequence of the definition of a ribbon Schur functor, but will be very useful later on:

Observation 4.41
.

Let
\alpha
,
\beta
, and
\gamma
be any (possibly empty) compositions. Given a Koszul algebra
A
and a Koszul left (resp. right)
A
-module
N
(resp.
M
), there is an equality

\mathbb{S}^{\alpha\cdot\beta\cdot\gamma}_{M,A,N}=\left(\mathbb{S}^{\alpha\cdot\beta}_{M,A}\otimes_{R}\mathbb{S}^{\gamma}_{A,N}\right)\cap\left(\mathbb{S}^{\alpha}_{M,A}\otimes_{R}\mathbb{S}^{\beta\cdot\gamma}_{A,N}\right),

where the intersection is being viewed as taking place in
(M\otimes_{R}A\otimes_{R}N)_{\alpha\cdot\beta\cdot\gamma}
.

Finally, we are able to tie ribbon Schur functors to the modules introduced in Definition
3.11
. This reformulation combined with the equivalence of Proposition
3.14
will yield quick proofs of the concatenation/near-concatenation formulation of Koszulness.

Observation 4.42
.

Let
\alpha
be any composition of length
\ell
. Given a Koszul algebra
A
and a Koszul left
A
-module
M
, there is an isomorphism of
R
-modules

\mathbb{S}^{\alpha}_{A,M}=L^{\phi^{-1}(\alpha)}_{S_{A,M,1}^{n},\dots,S_{A,M,n-1}^{n}},

where the map
\phi
is the isomorphism of posets of Definition
4.4
and the module
L^{I}_{M_{1},\dots,M_{n}}
is from Definition
3.11
. The analogous statement for right Koszul modules also holds.

Likewise, given a Koszul left (resp. right)
A
-module
N
(resp.
M
), there is an equality

\mathbb{S}^{\alpha}_{M,A,N}=L^{\phi^{-1}(\alpha)}_{S_{M,A,N,1}^{n},\dots,S_{M,A,N,n-1}^{n}}.

We arrive at the statement and proof of the relevant properties for ribbon Schur functors associated to Koszul algebras/modules; again, the proof here is deceptively short, but the proof combines all of the machinery developed thus far. Recall the notation for the reversal operator
\operatorname{rev}
from Observation
4.6
.

Lemma 4.43
.

Let
A
be a Koszul
R
-algebra and
M
(resp.
N
) a Koszul right (resp. left)
A
-module. Then:

(1)

For all compositions
\alpha
, the ribbon Schur module
\mathbb{S}^{\alpha}_{A,M}
(resp.
\mathbb{S}^{\alpha}_{N,A}
) is a flat
R
-module. If
A
and
M
are
R
-projective, then
\mathbb{S}^{\alpha}_{A,M}
(resp.
\mathbb{S}^{\alpha}_{N,A}
) is also
R
-projective.

(2)

For any two compositions
\alpha
and
\beta
there is a short exact sequence of
R
-modules

0\to\mathbb{S}^{\alpha\cdot\beta}_{A,M}\to\mathbb{S}^{\alpha}_{A}\otimes_{R}\mathbb{S}^{\beta}_{A,M}\to\mathbb{S}^{\alpha\odot\beta}_{A,M}\to 0

\text{resp.}\quad 0\to\mathbb{S}^{\alpha\cdot\beta}_{N,A}\to\mathbb{S}^{\alpha}_{N,A}\otimes_{R}\mathbb{S}^{\beta}_{A}\to\mathbb{S}^{\alpha\odot\beta}_{N,A}\to 0.

Conversely, if the sequences

0\to\mathbb{S}^{\alpha\cdot\beta}_{A,M_{\geqslant d}}\to\mathbb{S}^{\alpha}_{A}\otimes_{R}\mathbb{S}^{\beta}_{A,M_{\geqslant d}}\to\mathbb{S}^{\alpha\odot\beta}_{A,M_{\geqslant d}}\to 0

\text{resp.}\quad 0\to\mathbb{S}^{\alpha\cdot\beta}_{N_{\geqslant d},A}\to\mathbb{S}^{\alpha}_{N_{\geqslant d},A}\otimes_{R}\mathbb{S}^{\beta}_{A}\to\mathbb{S}^{\alpha\odot\beta}_{N_{\geqslant d},A}\to 0.

are exact for all integers
d\geqslant 0
and compositions
\alpha,\beta
, then
M
(resp.
N
) is a Koszul left (resp. right)
A
-module.

(3)

There is a canonical isomorphism of
R
-modules

\left(\mathbb{S}^{\alpha}_{A,M}\right)^{*}\cong\mathbb{S}^{\operatorname{rev}(\alpha^{t})}_{M^{!},A^{!}}

\text{resp.}\quad\left(\mathbb{S}^{\alpha}_{N,A}\right)^{*}\cong\mathbb{S}^{\operatorname{rev}(\alpha^{t})}_{A^{!},N^{!}}.

Remark 4.44
.

There is a convention here that is important to take note of in the short exact sequence of
(2)
: if the composition
\beta
is the empty composition, write
\alpha=\alpha^{\prime}\cdot(\alpha_{n})
; the short exact sequence
(2)
then reads

0\to\mathbb{S}^{\alpha}_{A,M}\to\mathbb{S}^{\alpha}_{A}\otimes_{R}M_{t}\to\mathbb{S}^{\alpha^{\prime}}_{A,M_{\geqslant t+\alpha_{n}}}\to 0.

There is a similar convention in the right module case: if
\alpha
is empty, write
\beta=(\beta_{1})\cdot\beta^{\prime}
. Then the short exact sequence reads

0\to\mathbb{S}^{\beta}_{N,A}\to N_{t}\otimes_{R}\mathbb{S}^{\beta}_{A}\to\mathbb{S}^{\beta^{\prime}}_{N_{\geqslant t+\beta_{1}},A}\to 0.

Notice moreover that the reason Lemma
4.23
(3) does not have the reversal
\operatorname{rev}(\alpha^{t})
is because there is by construction a canonical isomorphism
\mathbb{S}^{\alpha^{t}}_{A}\cong\mathbb{S}^{\operatorname{rev}(\alpha^{t})}_{A}
for any composition
\alpha
.

Proof.

Proof of (1):
This just combines Lemma
3.16
(1), Theorem
A.19
, and Observation
4.42
.

Proof of (2):
This is precisely the short exact sequence of Proposition
3.14
, so again the result follows from Theorem
A.19
combined with Observation
4.42
.

Proof of (3):
This just combines Lemma
3.16
(3), Theorem
A.19
, and Observation
4.42
.
∎

The case of having double module inputs in the associated Schur functor has to be treated separately, since the distributivity criterion cannot be used directly:

Corollary 4.45
.

Let
A
be a Koszul
R
-algebra and
N
(resp.
M
) any left (resp. right) Koszul
A
-module. Then:

(1)

For all compositions
\alpha
, the ribbon Schur module
\mathbb{S}^{\alpha}_{M,A,N}
is a flat
R
-module. If
A
,
M
, and
N
are
R
-projective, then
\mathbb{S}^{\alpha}_{M,A,N}
is
R
-projective.

(2)

For any two compositions
\alpha
and
\beta
there is a short exact sequence of
R
-modules

0\to\mathbb{S}^{\alpha\cdot\beta}_{M,A,N}\to\mathbb{S}^{\alpha}_{M,A}\otimes_{R}\mathbb{S}^{\beta}_{A,N}\to\mathbb{S}^{\alpha\odot\beta}_{M,A,N}\to 0.

(3)

There is a canonical isomorphism of
R
-modules

\left(\mathbb{S}^{\alpha}_{M,A,N}\right)^{*}\cong\mathbb{S}^{\operatorname{rev}(\alpha^{t})}_{N^{!},A^{!},M^{!}}.

Proof.

Notice that
(3)
follows from Lemma
3.16
(3) combined with Observation
4.42
.

Proof of (2):
As noted in Remark
3.15
, the sequence

0\to\mathbb{S}^{\alpha\cdot\beta}_{M,A,N}\to\mathbb{S}^{\alpha}_{M,A}\otimes_{R}\mathbb{S}^{\beta}_{A,N}\to\mathbb{S}^{\alpha\odot\beta}_{M,A,N}\to 0

is exact at the left and rightmost terms, so it suffices to prove exactness for the middle term. Assume first that
\ell(\alpha)=1
and
\ell(\beta)=0
. Then there is a commutative diagram:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{S}^{(\alpha_{1})}_{M,A,N}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{S}^{(\alpha_{1})}_{M,A}\otimes_{R}N_{s}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{M_{t}\otimes_{R}N_{s+\alpha_{1}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{M_{t}\otimes_{R}\mathbb{S}^{(\alpha_{1})}_{A,N}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{M_{t}\otimes_{R}A_{\alpha_{1}}\otimes_{R}N_{s}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{M_{t}\otimes_{R}N_{s+\alpha_{1}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{M_{t+\alpha_{1}}\otimes_{R}N_{s}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{M_{t+\alpha_{1}}\otimes_{R}N_{s}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}

The middle row and column is exact by Lemma
4.43
(2), and the last row and column are evidently exact. Let
\Theta
denote the surjection

\Theta:\mathbb{S}^{(\alpha_{1})}_{M,A}\otimes_{R}N_{s}\to M_{t}\otimes_{R}N_{s+\alpha_{1}}.

A quick diagram chase shows that

\operatorname{Ker}(\Theta)\subset\left(\mathbb{S}^{(\alpha_{1})}_{M,A}\otimes_{R}N_{s}\right)\cap\left(M_{t}\otimes_{R}\mathbb{S}^{(\alpha_{1})}_{A,N}\right),

and by Observation
4.41
this intersection is precisely the Schur module
\mathbb{S}^{(\alpha_{1})}_{M,A,N}
. Since the reverse inclusion evidently holds, the top row is exact.

Assume now that
\ell(\alpha),\ell(\beta)>0
. Write
\beta=\beta^{\prime}\cdot(\beta_{k})
for some composition
\beta^{\prime}
with
\ell(\beta^{\prime})=\ell(\beta)-1
. Then there is a commutative diagram:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{S}^{\alpha\cdot\beta}_{M,A,N}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{S}^{\alpha}_{M,A}\otimes_{R}\mathbb{S}^{\beta}_{A,N}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{S}^{\alpha\odot\beta}_{M,A,N}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{S}^{\alpha\cdot\beta^{\prime}}_{M,A}\otimes_{R}\mathbb{S}^{(\beta_{k})}_{A,N}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{S}^{\alpha}_{M,A}\otimes_{R}\mathbb{S}^{\beta^{\prime}}_{A}\otimes_{R}\mathbb{S}^{(\beta_{k})}_{A,N}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{S}^{\alpha\odot\beta^{\prime}}_{M,A}\otimes_{R}\mathbb{S}^{(\beta_{k})}_{A,N}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{S}^{\alpha\cdot\beta^{\prime}\odot(\beta_{k})}_{M,A,N}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{S}^{\alpha}_{M,A}\otimes_{R}\mathbb{S}^{\beta^{\prime}\odot(\beta_{k})}_{A.N}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{S}^{\alpha\odot\beta^{\prime}\odot(\beta_{k})}_{M,A,N}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}

The bottom two rows and the rightmost two columns are exact by induction (combined with the flatness proved in Lemma
4.43
), and an identical diagram chase as above shows that the top row must also be exact.

Proof of (1):
Proceed by induction on the length
\ell(\alpha)
, where the base cases
\ell(\alpha)=0
or
1
are evident since
\mathbb{S}^{\varnothing}_{M,A,N}:=M_{t}\otimes_{R}N_{s}
and there is a short exact sequence

0\to\mathbb{S}^{(\alpha_{1})}_{M,A,N}\to M_{t}\otimes_{R}\mathbb{S}^{(\alpha_{1})}_{A,N}\to M_{t+\alpha_{1}}\otimes_{R}N_{s}\to 0.

The latter two terms in this sequence are flat (resp. projective) by Lemma
4.43
, so
\mathbb{S}^{(\alpha_{1})}_{M,A,N}
is flat (resp. projective) by Observation
2.12
.

Assuming now that
\ell(\alpha)\geqslant 2
, write
\alpha=\beta\cdot\gamma
for two compositions
\beta
,
\gamma
with
\ell(\beta),\ell(\gamma)>0
. Then by
(2)
there is a short exact sequence

0\to\mathbb{S}^{\alpha}_{M,A,N}\to\mathbb{S}^{\beta}_{M,A}\otimes_{R}\mathbb{S}^{\gamma}_{A,M}\to\mathbb{S}^{\beta\odot\gamma}_{M,A,N}\to 0.

The latter two terms are flat (resp. projective) by the induction hypothesis, whence Observation
2.12
again implies that
\mathbb{S}^{\alpha}_{M,A,N}
is flat (resp. projective).
∎

Definition 4.46
.

Let
\alpha
be any
\ell
-partitioned composition. Given a Koszul algebra
A
and a Koszul left
A
-module
M
, define the cochain complex
(\mathcal{H}_{A,M}(\alpha),\delta)

whose
i^{th}
term is

\mathcal{H}^{i}_{A}(\alpha):=\bigoplus_{\begin{subarray}{c}I\subseteq[\ell]:\\
|I|=i\end{subarray}}\mathbb{S}^{\mu_{I}(\alpha)}_{A,M},\quad\text{with differential}

d^{\mathcal{H}_{A,M}}|_{\mathbb{S}^{\mu_{I}(\alpha)}}:=\sum_{j\notin I}\operatorname{sgn}(j,I)\rho_{I,I\cup j}.

Remark 4.47
.

Note the difference between the components of the modules for the complex of Definition
4.46
versus Definition
4.28
: in the first case, the direct sums are parametrized by subsets of
[\ell]
because of the additional component coming from the module
M
, whereas the components of Definition
4.28
only range over subsets of
[\ell-1]
since there is no additional module
M
.

Finally, we conclude this section with the analogue of the Hamel–Goulden type complexes of Definition
4.28
for ribbon Schur functors admitting a module input. Recall the notation of Definition
4.38
in the below:

Theorem 4.48
.

For all
\ell
-partitioned composition
\alpha
, the complex
\mathcal{H}_{A,M}(\alpha)
is a cochain complex with

H^{0}(\mathcal{H}_{A,M}(\alpha))\cong\mathbb{S}^{\alpha}_{A,M},

and which is exact in positive cohomological degrees.

Proof.

First, observe that the statement that
H^{0}(\mathcal{H}_{A,M}(\alpha))\cong\mathbb{S}^{\alpha}_{A,M}
is evidently true. Proceed by induction on
\ell
, with the base case
\ell=2
. In this case, write
\alpha=\beta\cdot\gamma
; the complex
\mathcal{H}_{A,M}(\alpha)
simply becomes the length
1
complex

\mathcal{H}_{A,M}(\alpha):\quad\mathbb{S}^{\beta}_{A}\otimes_{R}\mathbb{S}^{\gamma}_{A,M}\to\mathbb{S}^{\beta\odot\gamma}_{A,M}\to 0.

These are the last two terms of the short exact sequence from Lemma
4.43
(2), so the statement holds.

Assume now that
\ell(\alpha)>2
. Write
\alpha=\alpha^{\prime}\cdot p_{\ell}(\alpha)
. Then there is a short exact sequence of complexes:

0\to\mathcal{H}_{A,M}(\alpha^{\prime}\odot p_{\ell}(\alpha))[-1]\to\mathcal{H}_{A,M}(\alpha)\to\mathcal{H}_{A}(\alpha^{\prime})\otimes_{R}\mathbb{S}^{p_{\ell}(\alpha)}_{A,M}\to 0.

By the inductive hypothesis, both of the complexes
\mathcal{H}_{A,M}(\alpha^{\prime}\odot p_{\ell}(\alpha))
and
\mathcal{H}_{A}(\alpha^{\prime})\otimes_{R}\mathbb{S}^{p_{\ell}(\alpha)}_{A,M}
are exact in positive cohomological degrees; taking the long exact sequence of cohomology yields:

0\to\mathbb{S}^{\alpha}_{A,M}\to\mathbb{S}^{\alpha^{\prime}}_{A}\otimes_{R}\mathbb{S}^{p_{\ell}(\alpha)}_{A,M}\to\mathbb{S}^{\alpha^{\prime}\odot p_{\ell}(\alpha)}_{A,M}\to H^{1}(\mathcal{H}_{A,M}(\alpha))\to 0.

The map
\mathbb{S}^{\alpha^{\prime}}_{A}\otimes_{R}\mathbb{S}^{p_{\ell}(\alpha)}_{A,M}\to\mathbb{S}^{\alpha^{\prime}\odot p_{\ell}(\alpha)}_{A,M}
is precisely the map of Lemma
4.43
(2), which is of course surjective. Thus
H^{1}(\mathcal{H}_{A,M}(\alpha))=0
and the result follows.
∎

Recall that Example
4.31
gives an explicit example of the complex of Theorem
4.48
when just using the Schur modules
\mathbb{S}^{\alpha}_{A}
.

Example 4.49
.

As a more concrete example of Theorem
4.48
, let
A:=S(V)\cong{\mathbf{k}}[x_{1},\dots,x_{n}]
be the symmetric algebra (viewed as a polynomial ring) and
\mathfrak{m}:=(x_{1},\dots,x_{n})
the homogeneous maximal ideal. Given any composition
\alpha
and integer
d\geqslant 1
, notice that by definition there is an equality

\mathbb{S}^{\alpha}_{A,\mathfrak{m}^{d}}=\mathbb{S}^{\alpha\cdot(d)}_{A}.

Consider the composition
\alpha:=(1^{4})
for any integer. Then we can see the difference in the complex of Theorem
4.48
based on how we view the composition
\alpha
. Viewed as a
3
-partitioned composition, the complex of Theorem
4.48
yields

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{A_{1}\otimes_{R}A_{1}\otimes A_{1}\otimes_{R}A_{d}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\begin{matrix}A_{2}\otimes_{R}A_{1}\otimes_{R}A_{d}\\
\oplus\\
A_{1}\otimes_{R}A_{2}\otimes_{R}A_{d}\\
\oplus\\
A_{1}\otimes_{R}A_{1}\otimes_{R}A_{d+1}\end{matrix}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\begin{matrix}A_{3}\otimes_{R}A_{d}\\
\oplus\\
A_{2}\otimes_{R}A_{d+1}\\
\oplus\\
A_{1}\otimes_{R}A_{d+2}\end{matrix}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{A_{d+3}}}

This is simply the degree
d+3
homogeneous strand of the Bar complex on
\mathfrak{m}^{d}
. If we instead view
\alpha
as
2
-partitioned via
(1^{3})=(1^{2})\cdot(1)
, we obtain something distinct from a strand of the Bar complex:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{S}^{(1^{2})}_{A}\otimes_{R}A_{1}\otimes_{R}A_{d}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\begin{matrix}\mathbb{S}^{(1^{2})}_{A}\otimes_{R}A_{d+1}\\
\oplus\\
\mathbb{S}^{(1,2)}_{A}\otimes_{R}A_{d}\end{matrix}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{S}^{(1,d+2)}_{A}}}

Finally, we can also view
(1^{3})
as
1
-partitioned; recalling that
\mathbb{S}^{(1^{3})}_{A}=\bigwedge^{3}V
in this setting, Theorem
4.48
implies that there is a short exact sequence

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{S}^{(1^{3},d)}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\bigwedge^{3}V\otimes_{R}S^{d}(V)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{S}^{(1^{2},d+1)}_{A}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}

Note that
\mathbb{S}^{(1^{3},d)}_{A}
and
\mathbb{S}^{(1^{2},d+1)}_{A}
are equal to the classical defined Schur modules
\mathbb{S}^{(d,1^{3})}(V)
and
\mathbb{S}^{(d+1,1^{2})}(V)
, respectively, in which case the above short exact sequence recovers the well-known description of hook Schur modules as arising from homogeneous strands of the Koszul complex (see
[
BE75
]
). Thus Theorem
4.48
yields a family of complexes that interpolates between the full strands of the Bar complex and homogeneous strands of the Koszul complex.

5.
Multi-Schur Functors

In this section, we define multi-Schur functors. As mentioned in the introduction, the intuition behind these objects is that they are obtained by taking kernels of the defining ribbon Schur module relations diagonally; they will be particularly helpful for describing canonical equivariant decompositions of the derived invariants over Segre subalgebras.

Definition 5.1
.

Let
\alpha^{1},\dots,\alpha^{n}
be a sequence of compositions of fixed length
\ell
and
A^{1},\dots,A^{n}
be a sequence of Koszul
R
-algebras. The
multi-Schur module

\mathbb{S}^{\alpha^{1},\dots,\alpha^{n}}_{A^{1},\cdots,A^{n}}
is defined to be the kernel of the natural map

(A^{1}\otimes_{R}\cdots\otimes_{R}A^{n})_{(\alpha^{1},\dots,\alpha^{n})}\to\bigoplus_{i=1}^{\ell-1}(A^{1}\otimes_{R}\cdots\otimes_{R}A^{n})_{(\sigma_{i}(\alpha^{1}),\dots,\sigma_{i}(\alpha^{n}))}.

Given a sequence of Koszul left
A^{i}
-modules
M^{i}
of initial degree
t^{i}
for
1\leqslant i\leqslant n
, the
multi-Schur module

\mathbb{S}^{\alpha^{1},\dots,\alpha^{n}}_{(A^{1},M^{1}),\cdots,(A^{n},M^{n})}
is defined to be the kernel of the natural map

((A^{1}\otimes_{R}M^{1})\otimes_{R}\cdots\otimes_{R}(A^{n}\otimes_{R}M^{n}))_{(\alpha^{1}\cdot(t^{1}),\dots,\alpha^{n}\cdot(t^{n}))}

\to\bigoplus_{i=1}^{\ell-1}((A^{1}\otimes_{R}M^{1})\otimes_{R}\cdots\otimes_{R}(A^{n}\otimes_{R}M^{n}))_{(\sigma_{i}(\alpha^{1}\cdot(t^{1})),\dots,\sigma_{i}(\alpha^{n}\cdot(t^{n})))}.

The definition for a right
A
-module is analogous. Assume now that
\ell(\alpha^{1})\geqslant 2
. Given Koszul right (resp. left)
A
-modules
M^{1},\dots,M^{n}
(resp.
N^{1},\dots,N^{n}
) of initial degrees
t^{i}
(resp.
s^{i}
), the multi-Schur module

\mathbb{S}^{\alpha^{1},\dots,\alpha^{n}}_{(M^{1},A^{1},N^{1}),\cdots,(M^{n},A^{n},N^{n})}
is defined to be the kernel of the natural map

((M^{1}\otimes_{R}A^{1}\otimes_{R}N^{1})\otimes_{R}\cdots\otimes_{R}(M^{n}\otimes_{R}A^{n}\otimes_{R}N^{n}))_{((t^{1})\cdot\alpha^{1}\cdot(s^{1}),\dots,(t^{n})\cdot\alpha^{n}\cdot(s^{n}))}

\to\bigoplus_{i=1}^{\ell-1}((M^{1}\otimes_{R}A^{1}\otimes_{R}N^{1})\otimes_{R}\cdots\otimes_{R}(M^{n}\otimes_{R}A^{n}\otimes_{R}N^{n}))_{(\sigma_{i}((t^{1})\cdot\alpha^{1}\cdot(s^{1})),\dots,\sigma_{i}((t^{n})\cdot\alpha^{n}\cdot(s^{n})))}.

In a similar way to the ribbon Schur functors for single inputs, the multi-Schur modules can also be described as modules of the form
L^{I}_{M_{1},\dots,M_{n}}
for some collection of submodules, but the translation is a little more subtle:

Construction 5.2
.

Adopt notation and hypotheses as in Definition
5.1
, and recall the notation for the
R
-submodules
S_{A,N,i}^{j}
and
S_{M,A,N,i}^{j}
as introduced in Definitions
A.18
and
4.37
, respectively. For each of the compositions
\alpha^{i}
, there are isomorphisms of
R
-modules

(A^{i}\otimes_{R}N^{i})_{\alpha^{i}\cdot(s^{i})}\cong\frac{A_{1}^{i\otimes|\alpha^{i}|}\otimes_{R}N_{s^{i}}}{\vee_{j\in\phi^{-1}(\alpha^{i})}S_{A,N,j}^{|\alpha^{i}|}},

(M^{i}\otimes_{R}A^{i}\otimes_{R}N^{i})_{(t^{i})\cdot\alpha^{i}\cdot(s^{i})}\cong\frac{M_{t^{i}}\otimes_{R}A_{1}^{i\otimes|\alpha^{i}|}\otimes_{R}N_{s^{i}}}{\vee_{j\in\phi^{-1}(\alpha^{i})}S_{M,A,N,j}^{|\alpha^{i}|}}.

By the assumption that each
\alpha^{i}
has length
\ell
, each of the sets

[|\alpha^{i}|-1]\backslash\phi^{-1}(\alpha^{i})

has length
\ell-1
.
2
2

2

Put more informally: quotienting by the module
S_{A,i}^{n}
has the effect of deleting the
i
th comma in the composition
(1^{|\alpha|})
and replacing it with addition; if
\alpha
is
\ell
-partitioned, that means there are
\ell-1
commas
not
deleted from
(1^{|\alpha|})
.
Let
\psi_{\alpha^{i}}:[|\alpha^{i}|-1]\backslash\phi^{-1}(\alpha^{i})\to[\ell-1]
denote the unique order-preserving isomorphism between these two sets and consider the induced collections

S_{\underline{A},\underline{N},j}^{\underline{\alpha}}:=S_{A^{1},N^{1},\psi_{\alpha^{1}}^{-1}(j)}^{|\alpha^{1}|}+S_{A^{2},N^{2},\psi_{\alpha^{2}}^{-1}(j)}^{|\alpha^{2}|}+\cdots+S_{A^{n},N^{n},\psi_{\alpha^{n}}^{-1}(j)}^{|\alpha^{n}|}

\subset(A_{1}^{1\otimes|\alpha^{1}|}\otimes_{R}N^{1}_{s^{1}})\otimes_{R}(A_{1}^{2\otimes|\alpha^{2}|}\otimes_{R}N^{2}_{s^{2}})\otimes_{R}\cdots\otimes_{R}(A_{1}^{n\otimes|\alpha^{n}|}\otimes_{R}N^{n}_{s^{n}}),

S_{\underline{M},\underline{A},\underline{N},j}^{\underline{\alpha}}:=S_{M^{1},A^{1},N^{1},\psi_{\alpha^{1}}^{-1}(j)}^{|\alpha^{1}|}+S_{M^{2},A^{2},N^{2},\psi_{\alpha^{2}}^{-1}(j)}^{|\alpha^{2}|}+\cdots+S_{M^{n},A^{n},N^{n},\psi_{\alpha^{n}}^{-1}(j)}^{|\alpha^{n}|}

\subset(M^{1}_{t^{1}}\otimes_{R}A_{1}^{1\otimes|\alpha^{1}|}\otimes_{R}N^{1}_{s^{1}})\otimes_{R}(M^{2}_{t^{2}}\otimes_{R}A_{1}^{2\otimes|\alpha^{2}|}\otimes_{R}N^{2}_{s^{2}})\otimes_{R}\cdots\otimes_{R}(M^{n}_{t^{n}}\otimes_{R}A_{1}^{n\otimes|\alpha^{n}|}\otimes_{R}N^{n}_{s^{n}}).

In the above, we are abusing notation for sake of clarity: the module
S_{A^{i},N^{i},j}^{|\alpha^{i}|}
is defined as an
R
-submodule of
A_{1}^{i\otimes|\alpha^{i}|}\otimes_{R}N^{i}_{s^{i}}
, but in the above expressions we are vieweing each of these submodules as the
i
th tensor factor of

(A_{1}^{1\otimes|\alpha^{1}|}\otimes_{R}N^{1}_{s^{1}})\otimes_{R}(A_{1}^{2\otimes|\alpha^{2}|}\otimes_{R}N^{2}_{s^{2}})\otimes_{R}\cdots\otimes_{R}(A_{1}^{n\otimes|\alpha^{n}|}\otimes_{R}N^{n}_{s^{n}}).

The running flatness assumption implies that inclusion into the
i
th tensor factor is actually a well-defined injection. The same abuse of notation is used for
S_{M^{i},A^{i},N^{i},j}^{|\alpha^{i}|}
.

Observation 5.3
.

Adopt notation and hypotheses as in Construction
5.2
. Then there are isomorphisms of
R
-modules:

\mathbb{S}^{\alpha^{1},\dots,\alpha^{n}}_{(A^{1},N^{1}),\dots,(A^{n},N^{n})}\cong

\frac{\wedge_{j\in[\ell-1]}S^{\underline{\alpha}}_{\underline{A},\underline{M}}}{\vee_{j\notin\phi^{-1}(\alpha^{1})}S_{A^{1},N^{1},j}^{|\alpha^{1}|}+\cdots+\vee_{j\notin\phi^{-1}(\alpha^{n})}S_{A^{n},N^{n},j}^{|\alpha^{n}|}},

\mathbb{S}^{\alpha^{1},\dots,\alpha^{n}}_{(M^{1},A^{1},N^{1}),\dots,(M^{n},A^{n},N^{n})}\cong

\frac{\wedge_{j\in[\ell-1]}S^{\underline{\alpha}}_{\underline{M},\underline{A},\underline{M}}}{\vee_{j\notin\phi^{-1}(\alpha^{1})}S_{M^{1},A^{1},N^{1},j}^{|\alpha^{1}|}+\cdots+\vee_{j\notin\phi^{-1}(\alpha^{n})}S_{M^{n},A^{n},N^{n},j}^{|\alpha^{n}|}}.

An interesting aspect of multi-Schur modules is that one can define the multi-Schur modules when the input compositions have different sizes; to make sense of this, the inputs need to be
\ell
-partitioned into the same number of parts, instead:

Definition 5.4
.

Let
\alpha^{1},\dots,\alpha^{n}
be a sequence of
\ell
-partitioned compositions and
A^{1},\dots,A^{n}
be a sequence of Koszul
R
-algebras. The
multi-Schur module

\mathbb{S}^{\alpha^{1},\dots,\alpha^{n}}_{A^{1},\cdots,A^{n}}
is defined as the kernel of the natural map:

\mathbb{S}^{\mu_{\varnothing}(\alpha^{1})}_{A^{1}}\otimes_{R}\mathbb{S}^{\mu_{\varnothing}(\alpha^{2})}_{A^{2}}\otimes_{R}\cdots\otimes_{R}\mathbb{S}^{\mu_{\varnothing}(\alpha^{n})}_{A^{n}}\to\bigoplus_{i=1}^{\ell-1}\mathbb{S}^{\mu_{i}(\alpha^{1})}_{A^{1}}\otimes_{R}\mathbb{S}^{\mu_{i}(\alpha^{2})}_{A^{2}}\otimes_{R}\cdots\otimes_{R}\mathbb{S}^{\mu_{i}(\alpha^{n})}_{A^{n}}.

If
\alpha:=\alpha^{1}=\alpha^{2}=\cdots=\alpha^{n}
then the more concise notation
\mathbb{S}^{\alpha}_{A^{1},\dots,A^{n}}
will be used to denote
\mathbb{S}^{\alpha^{1},\dots,\alpha^{n}}_{A^{1},\dots,A^{n}}
.

Likewise, given a Koszul left
A
-module
M
, define the multi-Schur module
\mathbb{S}^{\alpha^{1},\dots,\alpha^{n}}_{(A^{1},M^{1}),\cdots,(A^{n},M^{n})}
as the kernel of the natural map:

\mathbb{S}^{\mu_{\varnothing}(\alpha^{1})}_{(A^{1},M^{1})}\otimes_{R}\mathbb{S}^{\mu_{\varnothing}(\alpha^{2})}_{(A^{2},M^{2})}\otimes_{R}\cdots\otimes_{R}\mathbb{S}^{\mu_{\varnothing}(\alpha^{n})}_{(A^{n},M^{n})}\to\bigoplus_{i=1}^{\ell}\mathbb{S}^{\mu_{i}(\alpha^{1})}_{(A^{1},M^{1})}\otimes_{R}\mathbb{S}^{\mu_{i}(\alpha^{2})}_{(A^{2},M^{2})}\otimes_{R}\cdots\otimes_{R}\mathbb{S}^{\mu_{i}(\alpha^{n})}_{(A^{n},M^{n})}.

Finally, given a Koszul left (resp. right)
A
-module
N
(resp.
M
), define the multi-Schur module

\mathbb{S}^{\alpha^{1},\dots,\alpha^{n}}_{(M^{1},A^{1},N^{1}),\cdots,(M^{n},A^{n},N^{n})}

as the kernel of the natural map:

\mathbb{S}^{\mu_{\varnothing}(\alpha^{1})}_{(M^{1},A^{1},N^{1})}\otimes_{R}\cdots\otimes_{R}\mathbb{S}^{\mu_{\varnothing}(\alpha^{n})}_{(M^{n},A^{n},N^{n})}\to\bigoplus_{i=1}^{\ell}\mathbb{S}^{\mu_{i}(\alpha^{1})}_{(M^{1},A^{1},N^{1})}\otimes_{R}\cdots\otimes_{R}\mathbb{S}^{\mu_{i}(\alpha^{n})}_{(M^{n},A^{n},N^{n})}.

The following is the evident analogue of Observation
4.41
for multi-Schur modules:

Observation 5.5
.

Let
\alpha^{i},\beta^{i}
, and
\gamma^{i}
be
\ell
(resp.
j
,
k
)-partitioned partitions. Given a collection of Koszul algebras
A^{i}
and left (resp. right) Koszul
A^{i}
-modules
N_{i}
(resp.
M_{i}
) for
1\leqslant i\leqslant n
, there is an equality

\mathbb{S}^{\alpha^{1}\cdot\beta^{1}\cdot\gamma^{1},\dots,\alpha^{n}\cdot\beta^{n}\cdot\gamma^{n}}_{(M^{1},A^{1},N^{1}),\cdots,(M^{n},A^{n},N^{n})}=

\left(\mathbb{S}^{\alpha^{1}\cdot\beta^{1},\dots,\alpha^{n}\cdot\beta^{n}}_{(M^{1},A^{1},N^{1}),\cdots,(M^{n},A^{n},N^{n})}\otimes_{R}\mathbb{S}^{\gamma^{1},\dots,\gamma^{n}}_{(M^{1},A^{1},N^{1}),\cdots,(M^{n},A^{n},N^{n})}\right)

\cap\left(\mathbb{S}^{\alpha^{1},\dots,\alpha^{n}}_{(M^{1},A^{1},N^{1}),\cdots,(M^{n},A^{n},N^{n})}\otimes_{R}\mathbb{S}^{\beta^{1}\cdot\gamma^{1},\dots,\beta^{n}\cdot\gamma^{n}}_{(M^{1},A^{1},N^{1}),\cdots,(M^{n},A^{n},N^{n})}\right).

The following properties are immediate from the definition of multi-Schur modules:

Proposition 5.6
.

Let
\alpha^{1},\dots,\alpha^{n}
be a sequence of
\ell
-partitioned compositions and
A^{1},\dots,A^{n}
be a sequence of Koszul
R
-algebras. Then:

(1)

Let
\tau\in\Sigma_{n}
be any permutation of
[n]
. Then there is a natural isomorphism

\mathbb{S}^{\alpha^{1},\dots,\alpha^{n}}_{A^{1},\dots,A^{n}}\cong\mathbb{S}^{\alpha^{\tau(1)},\dots,\alpha^{\tau(n)}}_{A^{\tau(1)},\dots,A^{\tau(n)}}.

(2)

If
\ell=1
, there is an isomorphism

\mathbb{S}^{\alpha^{1},\dots,\alpha^{n}}_{A^{1},\dots,A^{n}}\cong\mathbb{S}^{\alpha^{1}}_{A^{1}}\otimes_{R}\cdots\otimes_{R}\mathbb{S}^{\alpha^{n}}_{A^{n}}.

(3)

If each
A^{i}
admits the structure of a
R[G^{i}]
-module for some group
G^{i}
(where
1\leqslant i\leqslant n
), then the multi-Schur module
\mathbb{S}^{\alpha^{1},\dots,\alpha^{n}}_{A^{1},\dots,A^{n}}
admits the structure of a
R[G^{1}\times\cdots\times G^{n}]
-module, and all of the above isomorphisms are
G^{1}\times\cdots\times G^{n}
-equivariant.

In view of Observation
5.5
, the notation for multi-Schur modules can quickly become overwhelming. For this reason, we introduce the following shorthand notation:

Notation 5.7
.

Let
\alpha^{1},\dots,\alpha^{n}
be a collection of
\ell
-partitioned compositions. Given a collection of Koszul algebras
A^{i}
and left (resp. right) Koszul
A^{i}
-modules
N_{i}
(resp.
M_{i}
) for
1\leqslant i\leqslant n
, use the more concise notation

\mathbb{S}^{\underline{\alpha}}_{\underline{M},\underline{A},\underline{N}}:=\mathbb{S}^{\alpha^{1},\dots,\alpha^{n}}_{(M^{1},A^{1},N^{1}),\cdots,(M^{n},A^{n},^{n})}.

For given tuples of compositions
\underline{\alpha}
and
\underline{\beta}
, extend the operations of Definitions
4.4
and
4.1
by applying them coordinate-wise to the tuples. With this identification, we have the equalities

\underline{\alpha}:=\{\alpha^{1},\dots,\alpha^{n}\},\quad\underline{M}:=\{M^{1},\dots,M^{n}\},

\underline{A}:=\{A^{1},\dots,A^{n}\},\quad\underline{N}:=\{N^{1},\dots,N^{n}\}.

The following lemma is the multi-Schur analog of Lemma
4.43
, but the proof is actually a little more subtle due to the added difficulty of allowing compositions of different sizes.

Lemma 5.8
.

Let
\underline{\alpha}
and
\underline{\beta}
be sequences of
k
-partitioned and
\ell-k
-partitioned compositions, respectively, for some fixed
1\leqslant k\leqslant\ell
. Let
\underline{A}=\{A^{1},\dots,A^{n}\}
be a sequence of Koszul
R
-algebras and
\underline{M}
a sequence of Koszul left
\underline{A}
-modules. Then:

(1)

Every multi-Schur module is
R
-flat.

(2)

There is a canonical short exact sequence

0\to\mathbb{S}^{\underline{\alpha}\cdot\underline{\beta}}_{\underline{A},\underline{M}}\to\mathbb{S}^{\underline{\alpha}}_{\underline{A}}\otimes_{R}\mathbb{S}^{\underline{\beta}}_{\underline{A},\underline{M}}\to\mathbb{S}^{\underline{\alpha}\odot\underline{\beta}}_{\underline{A},\underline{M}}\to 0.

If all Koszul algebras/modules are
R
-projective, then every multi-Schur module
\mathbb{S}^{\underline{\alpha}}_{\underline{A},\underline{M}}
is also
R
-projective. The analogous statement for right modules holds as well.

Proof.

It suffices to prove
(2)
, since
(1)
is a consequence of
(2)
combined with Observation
2.12
.

Proof of (2):
Let
p:=\max\{\ell(p_{i}(\alpha^{j}))\mid 1\leqslant i\leqslant k,\ 1\leqslant j\leqslant n\}
and define
q:=|\{i\mid\ell(p_{i}(\alpha^{j}))=p\}|
. The proof is by a double induction on the values
p
and
q
. Notice that when
p=1
and
q
is arbitrary, recall that
A^{1}\otimes_{R}\cdots\otimes_{R}A^{n}
is a Koszul algebra and the tensor product
M^{1}\otimes_{R}\cdots\otimes_{R}M^{n}
is a left Koszul module over
A^{1}\otimes_{R}\cdots\otimes_{R}A^{n}
by Corollary
A.23
. By Observation
5.3
combined with Proposition
3.14
, the sequence of
(2)
is exact.

Assume now that
p>1
and
q=1
. This means that there is some element of one of the compositions
\underline{\alpha}
or
\underline{\beta}
whose largest part is strictly greater than
1
. Reversing the order of
\alpha
and
\beta
, it is of no loss of generality to assume that
\underline{\alpha}
contains a part of size
p
. Moreover, for simplicity of notation, let us assume that the first element of
\underline{\alpha}
has size
p
(the general case is identical but notationally more cumbersome). Write
\underline{\alpha}=\underline{(a)}\cdot\underline{\alpha^{\prime}}
, so that by construction
\underline{\alpha^{\prime}}
has all parts of size
<p
. There is a commutative diagram:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{S}^{\underline{\alpha}\cdot\underline{\beta}}_{\underline{A},\underline{M}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{S}^{\underline{\alpha}}_{\underline{A}}\otimes_{R}\mathbb{S}^{\underline{\beta}}_{\underline{A},\underline{M}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{S}^{\underline{\alpha}\odot\underline{\beta}}_{\underline{A},\underline{M}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{S}^{\underline{(a)}}_{\underline{A}}\otimes_{R}\mathbb{S}^{\underline{\alpha^{\prime}}\cdot\underline{\beta}}_{\underline{A},\underline{M}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{S}^{\underline{(a)}}_{\underline{A}}\otimes_{R}\mathbb{S}^{\underline{\alpha^{\prime}}}_{\underline{A}}\otimes_{R}\mathbb{S}^{\underline{\beta}}_{\underline{A},\underline{M}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{S}^{\underline{(a)}}_{\underline{A}}\otimes_{R}\mathbb{S}^{\underline{\alpha^{\prime}}\odot\underline{\beta}}_{\underline{A},\underline{M}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{S}^{\underline{(a)}\odot\underline{\alpha^{\prime}}\cdot\underline{\beta}}_{\underline{A},\underline{M}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{S}^{\underline{(a)}\odot\underline{\alpha^{\prime}}}_{\underline{A}}\otimes_{R}\mathbb{S}^{\underline{\beta}}_{\underline{A},\underline{M}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{S}^{\underline{(a)}\odot\underline{\alpha^{\prime}}\odot\underline{\beta}}_{\underline{A},\underline{M}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}

In the above diagram, notice that the bottom two rows and the last two columns are exact by the inductive hypothesis. We may also assume by induction on
p
that the map

\Theta:\mathbb{S}^{\underline{(a)}}_{\underline{A}}\otimes_{R}\mathbb{S}^{\underline{\alpha^{\prime}}\cdot\underline{\beta}}_{\underline{A},\underline{M}}\to\mathbb{S}^{\underline{(a)}\odot\underline{\alpha^{\prime}}\cdot\underline{\beta}}_{\underline{A},\underline{M}}

is a surjection. We claim that with this information the first column of the above diagram is exact. It is evident just by definition that the map

\mathbb{S}^{\underline{(a)}\cdot\underline{\alpha^{\prime}}\cdot\underline{\beta}}_{\underline{A},\underline{M}}\to\mathbb{S}^{\underline{\alpha}}_{\underline{A}}\otimes_{R}\mathbb{S}^{\underline{\beta}}_{\underline{A},\underline{M}}

is always an injection, so it remains to prove exactness at the middle term
\mathbb{S}^{\underline{\alpha}}_{\underline{A}}\otimes_{R}\mathbb{S}^{\underline{\beta}}_{\underline{A},\underline{M}}
. However, a diagram chase employing exactness of the middle column shows that

\operatorname{Ker}\Theta\subset\left(\mathbb{S}^{\underline{\alpha}}_{\underline{A}}\otimes_{R}\mathbb{S}^{\underline{\beta}}_{\underline{A},\underline{M}}\right)\cap\left(\mathbb{S}^{\underline{(a)}}_{\underline{A}}\otimes_{R}\mathbb{S}^{\underline{\alpha^{\prime}}\cdot\underline{\beta}}_{\underline{A},\underline{M}}\right),

and this latter intersection is precisely equal to
\mathbb{S}^{\underline{\alpha}\cdot\underline{\beta}}_{\underline{A},\underline{M}}
by Observation
5.5
. Since the reverse containment evidently holds, exactness of the first column follows, and hence all columns of the above diagram are exact. Employing the long exact sequence of homology, it follows that the first row is exact.

For the inductive step on
q
, the argument is actually identical. After choosing a similar decomposition of
\alpha
, there is an identical commutative diagram. By construction, the bottom two rows and rightmost two columns are exact by induction on
q
. A verbatim argument works for showing that the first column is exact, and hence the long exact sequence of homology yields the statement in general.
∎

Corollary 5.9
.

Let
\underline{\alpha}
and
\underline{\beta}
be a collection of
k
and
\ell-k
-partitioned compositions, respectively. Let
A^{i}
be a collection of Koszul algebras and
N_{i}
(resp.
M_{i}
) a collection of left (resp. right) Koszul
A^{i}
-modules for
1\leqslant i\leqslant n
. Then:

(1)

Every multi-Schur module
\mathbb{S}^{\underline{\alpha}}_{\underline{M},\underline{A},\underline{N}}
is
R
-flat.

(2)

There is a canonical short exact sequence

0\to\mathbb{S}^{\underline{\alpha}\cdot\underline{\beta}}_{\underline{M},\underline{A},\underline{N}}\to\mathbb{S}^{\underline{\alpha}}_{\underline{M},\underline{A}}\otimes_{R}\mathbb{S}^{\underline{\beta}}_{\underline{A},\underline{N}}\to\mathbb{S}^{\underline{\alpha}\odot\underline{\beta}}_{\underline{M},\underline{A},\underline{N}}\to 0.

If all the Koszul algebras/modules are
R
-projective, then every multi-Schur module
\mathbb{S}^{\underline{\alpha}}_{\underline{A},\underline{M}}
is also
R
-projective.

Proof.

This proof is formally identical to the proof of Corollary
4.45
but with the multi-index notation of Notation
5.7
used instead.
∎

Finally, we have the generalization of the complexes
\mathcal{H}(\alpha)
of Definition
4.46
for the multi-Schur setting:

Definition 5.10
.

Let
\alpha^{1},\dots,\alpha^{n}
be a sequence of
\ell
-partitioned compositions. Given Koszul algebras
A^{i}
and left Koszul modules
M^{i}
for
1\leqslant i\leqslant n
, define the cochain complex
\mathcal{H}_{(A^{1},M^{1}),\dots,(A^{n},M^{n})}^{\bullet}(\alpha^{1},\dots,\alpha^{n})
via

\mathcal{H}_{(A^{1},M^{1}),\dots,(A^{n},M^{n})}^{i}(\alpha^{1},\dots,\alpha^{n}):=\bigoplus_{|I|=i}\mathbb{S}^{\mu_{I}(\alpha^{1}),\dots,\mu_{I}(\alpha^{n})}_{(A^{1},M^{1}),\cdots,(A^{n},M^{n})},\quad\text{with differential}

d^{\mathcal{H}_{(A^{1},M^{1}),\dots,(A^{n},M^{n})}}|_{\mathbb{S}^{\mu_{I}(\alpha^{1}),\dots,\mu_{I}(\alpha^{n})}_{A^{1},\cdots,A^{n}}}:=\sum_{j\notin I}\operatorname{sgn}(j,I)\rho_{I,I\cup j}.

Corollary 5.11
.

Let
\alpha^{1},\dots,\alpha^{n}
be a sequence of
\ell
-partitioned compositions. Given Koszul algebras
A^{i}
and left Koszul modules
M^{i}
for
1\leqslant i\leqslant n
, the cochain complex
\mathcal{H}^{\bullet}_{(A^{1},M^{1}),\dots,(A^{n},M^{n})}(\alpha^{1},\dots,\alpha^{n})
is exact in positive cohomological degrees and

H^{0}\left(\mathcal{H}^{\bullet}_{(A^{1},M^{1}),\dots,(A^{n},M^{n})}(\alpha^{1},\dots,\alpha^{n})\right)=\mathbb{S}^{\alpha^{1},\dots,\alpha^{n}}_{A^{1},\cdots,A^{n}}.

5.1.
Some Generalities on Filtrations

We now turn our attention to the task of filtering the multi-Schur modules
\mathbb{S}^{\underline{\alpha}}_{\underline{M},\underline{A},\underline{N}}
for given choices of
\underline{\alpha}
. This section collects a few general results on “splicing” filtrations together; these results are essentially trivial, but it will be useful to refer to them explicitly. First, let us recall the definition of a filtration.

Definition 5.12
.

Let
M
be an
R
-module. A (ascending)
filtration
of
M
is a chain of
R
-submodules

0=F_{0}\subset F_{1}\subset\cdots\subset F_{n-1}\subset F_{n}=M.

The successive quotients
F_{i}/F_{i-1}
are called the
associated graded pieces
.

The following observation is likely a common first exercise on properties of filtrations, but we state and prove it here for completeness. The intuition here is that filtrations whose graded pieces admit further filtrations may be refined to a single filtration of the larger object with the same graded pieces.

Observation 5.13
.

Let
M
be an
R
-module equipped with a finite filtration

F:\quad 0=F_{0}\subset F_{1}\subset\cdots\subset F_{n-1}\subset F_{n}=M.

Suppose that for all
i\geqslant 1
, the
i
th graded piece
G_{i}:=F_{i}/F_{i-1}
admits a finite filtration

F^{i}:\quad 0=F_{0}^{i}\subset F_{1}^{i}\subset\cdots\subset F_{n_{i}-1}^{i}\subset F_{n_{i}}^{i}=G_{i}

with graded pieces
G_{j}^{i}:=F_{j}^{i}/F_{j-1}^{i}
for each
j\geqslant 1
. Then the filtration
F
of
M
may be refined to a filtration
F^{\prime}
of
M
with associated graded pieces
G_{j}^{i}
for
1\leqslant i\leqslant n
,
1\leqslant j\leqslant n_{i}
.

The above observation may be used to understand how to filter tensor products of modules, each equipped separately with their own filtrations; this is the content of the following corollary.

Corollary 5.14
.

Let
M^{1},M^{2},\dots,M^{\ell}
be a collection of
R
-modules equipped with filtrations

F^{i}:\quad\quad 0=F_{0}^{i}\subset F_{1}^{i}\subset\cdots\subset F_{n_{i}-1}^{i}\subset F_{n_{i}}^{i}=M^{i},

and assume that each associated graded piece
G_{j}^{i}:=F_{j}^{i}/F_{j-1}^{i}
is a flat
R
-module. Then each of the
R
-modules
M^{i}
is flat and there is a filtration of
M^{1}\otimes_{R}M^{2}\otimes_{R}\cdots\otimes_{R}M^{\ell}
with associated graded pieces of the form

G_{i_{1}}^{1}\otimes_{R}G_{i_{2}}^{2}\otimes_{R}\cdots\otimes_{R}G_{i_{\ell}}^{\ell},

where
1\leqslant i_{k}\leqslant n_{k}
for each
1\leqslant k\leqslant\ell
.

Proof.

Proceed by induction on
\ell
, with the base case
\ell=1
being vacuous. Assume
\ell>1
and let
F^{\prime}
be a filtration of
M^{1}\otimes_{R}M^{2}\otimes_{R}\cdots\otimes_{R}M^{\ell-1}
with associated graded pieces of the form

G_{i_{1}}^{1}\otimes_{R}\cdots\otimes_{R}G_{i_{\ell-1}}^{\ell-1}.

By the flatness assumption, the induced filtration
F^{\prime}\otimes_{R}M^{\ell}
has associated graded pieces of the form

G_{i_{1}}^{1}\otimes_{R}\cdots\otimes_{R}G_{i_{\ell-1}}^{\ell-1}\otimes_{R}M^{\ell}.

Further filtering each graded piece by the filtration
F^{\ell}
of
M^{\ell}
, we may employ Observation
5.13
to deduce the result.
∎

5.2.
A Canonical Filtration of Multi-Schur Functors

The following short exact sequence, when combined with some of the other filtration results proved in this subsection, will be the essential ingredient for proving Theorem
5.20
. It will be used to place an object we want to understand in the middle of a short exact sequence whose outer terms have a well-understood filtration.

Lemma 5.15
.

Let
\alpha^{1},\dots,\alpha^{n}
and
\beta^{1},\dots,\beta^{n}
be sequences of
k
-partitioned and
\ell-k
-partitioned compositions, respectively, for some fixed
0\leqslant k<\ell
. Let
A^{1},\dots,A^{n}
be a sequence of Koszul
R
-algebras and
N^{i}
(resp.
M^{i}
) be Koszul left (resp. right)
A^{i}
-modules for
1\leqslant i\leqslant n
. Then there is a canonical short exact sequence

0\to\mathbb{S}^{\alpha^{1}\cdot\beta^{1},\dots,\alpha^{n-1}\cdot\beta^{n-1},\alpha^{n}}_{\underline{M},\underline{A},\underline{N}}\otimes_{R}\mathbb{S}^{\beta^{n}}_{M^{n},A^{n},N^{n}}

\to\mathbb{S}^{\alpha^{1}\cdot\beta^{1},\dots,\alpha^{n}\cdot\beta^{n}}_{\underline{M},\underline{A},\underline{N}}\to\mathbb{S}^{\alpha^{1}\odot\beta^{1},\dots,\alpha^{n-1}\odot\beta^{n-1},\alpha^{n}\cdot\beta^{n}}_{\underline{M},\underline{A},\underline{N}}\to 0.

In the above, the sequences of
\alpha^{1}\cdot\beta^{1},\dots,\alpha^{n-1}\cdot\beta^{n-1},\alpha^{n}
and
\alpha^{1}\odot\beta^{1},\dots,\alpha^{n-1}\odot\beta^{n-1},\alpha^{n}\cdot\beta^{n}
are being viewed as
k
and
\ell-1
-partitioned compositions via the convention of Convention
4.14
.

Example 5.16
.

Let
A
and
B
be two Koszul
R
-algebras and
\alpha^{1}=\beta^{1}=(1^{3})
, viewed as a
3
-partitioned composition. Then the short exact sequence of Lemma
5.15
takes the form

\mathbb{S}^{(1^{3}),(1^{2})}_{A,B}\otimes_{R}B_{1}\to\mathbb{S}^{(1^{3}),(1^{3})}_{A,B}\to\mathbb{S}^{(1,2),(1^{3})}_{A,B}\to 0.

In the above,
(1^{3})
is being viewed as the
2
-partitioned composition
(1)\cdot(1^{2})
in the first term of the sequence, and likewise for the last term in the sequence.

Proof.

For simplicity of notation, we will prove the theorem only for Koszul algebra inputs (otherwise the relevant diagrams are too large to display). The proof follows by examining the commutative diagram of Figure
3
; the base case and the inductive step are outlined in the caption.

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{S}^{\alpha^{1}\cdot\beta^{1},\dots,\alpha^{n-1}\cdot\beta^{n-1},\alpha^{n}}_{A^{1},\cdots,A^{n}}\otimes_{R}\mathbb{S}^{\beta^{n}}_{A^{n}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{S}^{\alpha^{1}\cdot\beta^{1},\dots,\alpha^{n-1}\cdot\beta^{n-1},\alpha^{1}}_{A^{1},\cdots,A^{n}}\otimes_{R}\mathbb{S}^{\beta^{n}}_{A^{n}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{S}^{\alpha^{1}\cdot\beta^{1},\dots,\alpha^{n-1}\cdot\beta^{n-1},\alpha^{n}\cdot\beta^{n}}_{A^{1},\cdots,A^{n}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{S}^{\alpha^{1},\dots,\alpha^{n}}_{A^{1},\cdots,A^{n}}\otimes_{R}\mathbb{S}^{\beta^{1},\dots,\beta^{n}}_{A^{1},\cdots,A^{n}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{S}^{\alpha^{1}\odot\beta^{1},\dots,\alpha^{n-1}\odot\beta^{n-1},\alpha^{n}\odot\beta^{n}}_{A^{1},\cdots,A^{n}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{S}^{\alpha^{1}\odot\beta^{1},\dots,\alpha^{n-1}\odot\beta^{n-1},\alpha^{n}\cdot\beta^{n}}_{A^{1},\cdots,A^{n}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{S}^{\alpha^{1}\odot\beta^{1},\dots,\alpha^{n-1}\odot\beta^{n-1}}_{A^{1},\cdots,A^{n-1}}\otimes_{R}\mathbb{S}^{\alpha^{n}}_{A^{n}}\otimes_{R}\mathbb{S}^{\beta^{n}}_{A^{n}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{S}^{\alpha^{1}\odot\beta^{1},\dots,\alpha^{n-1}\odot\beta^{n-1},\alpha^{n}\odot\beta^{n}}_{A^{1},\cdots,A^{n}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{S}^{\alpha^{1}\cdot\beta^{1}\cdot\gamma^{1},\dots,\alpha^{n-1}\cdot\beta^{n-1}\cdot\gamma^{n-1},\alpha^{n}\cdot\beta^{n}}_{A^{1},\cdots,A^{n}}\otimes_{R}\mathbb{S}^{\gamma^{n}}_{A^{n}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\begin{matrix}\mathbb{S}^{\alpha^{1},\dots,\alpha^{n}}_{A^{1},\cdots,A^{n}}\otimes_{R}\\
\mathbb{S}^{\beta^{1}\cdot\gamma^{1},\dots,\beta^{n-1}\cdot\gamma^{n-1},\beta^{n}}_{A^{1},\cdots,A^{n}}\otimes_{R}\mathbb{S}^{\gamma^{n}}_{A^{n}}\end{matrix}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{S}^{\alpha^{1}\odot\beta^{1}\cdot\gamma^{1},\dots,\alpha^{n-1}\odot\beta^{n-1}\cdot\gamma^{n-1},\alpha^{n}\odot\beta^{n}}_{A^{1},\cdots,A^{n}}\otimes_{R}\mathbb{S}^{\gamma^{n}}_{A^{n}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{S}^{\alpha^{1}\cdot\beta^{1}\cdot\gamma^{1},\dots,\alpha^{n-1}\cdot\beta^{n-1}\cdot\gamma^{n-1},\alpha^{n}\cdot\beta^{n}\cdot\gamma^{n}}_{A^{1},\cdots,A^{n}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\begin{matrix}\mathbb{S}^{\alpha^{1},\dots,\alpha^{n}}_{A^{1},\cdots,A^{n}}\otimes_{R}\\
\mathbb{S}^{\beta^{1}\cdot\gamma^{1},\dots,\beta^{n-1}\cdot\gamma^{n-1},\beta^{n}\cdot\gamma^{n}}_{A^{1},\cdots,A^{n}}\end{matrix}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{S}^{\alpha^{1}\odot\beta^{1}\cdot\gamma^{1},\dots,\alpha^{n-1}\odot\beta^{n-1}\cdot\gamma^{n-1},\alpha^{n}\odot\beta^{n}\cdot\gamma^{n}}_{A^{1},\cdots,A^{n}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{S}^{\alpha^{1}\cdot\beta^{1}\odot\gamma^{1},\dots,\alpha^{n-1}\cdot\beta^{n-1}\odot\gamma^{n-1},\alpha^{n}\cdot\beta^{n}\cdot\gamma^{n}}_{A^{1},\cdots,A^{n}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\begin{matrix}\mathbb{S}^{\alpha^{1},\dots,\alpha^{n}}_{A^{1},\cdots,A^{n}}\otimes_{R}\\
\mathbb{S}^{\beta^{1}\odot\gamma^{1},\dots,\beta^{n-1}\odot\gamma^{n-1},\beta^{n}\cdot\gamma^{n}}_{A^{1},\cdots,A^{n}}\end{matrix}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{S}^{\alpha^{1}\odot\beta^{1}\odot\gamma^{1},\dots,\alpha^{n-1}\odot\beta^{n-1}\odot\gamma^{n-1},\alpha^{n}\odot\beta^{n}\cdot\gamma^{n}}_{A^{1},\cdots,A^{n}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}

Figure 3.
The commutative diagrams used to prove Lemma
5.15
. In the first diagram, all rows are exact (the middle is exact by Lemma
5.8
. Likewise, the third column is clearly exact and the middle column is exact by Lemma
5.8
, so the first column is exact by the long exact sequence of homology. For the second diagram, all of the rows are exact by Lemma
5.8
, and the second and third columns are exact according to the inductive hypothesis. By the long exact sequence of homology, the first column must also be exact.

∎

The next lemma can be seen as a “first approximation” of the filtration in Theorem
5.20
.

Lemma 5.17
.

Let
\alpha^{1},\dots,\alpha^{n}
be a sequence of
\ell
-partitioned compositions and
A^{1},\dots,A^{n}
be a sequence of Koszul
R
-algebras and
N^{i}
(resp.
M^{i}
) be Koszul left (resp. right)
A^{i}
-modules for
1\leqslant i\leqslant n
. Then the multi-Schur module
\mathbb{S}^{\underline{\alpha}}_{\underline{M},\underline{A},\underline{N}}
admits a canonical filtration with graded pieces of the form

\mathbb{S}^{\sigma_{I}(\alpha^{1}),\dots,\sigma_{I}(\alpha^{n-1})}_{(M^{1},A^{1},N^{1}),\dots,(M^{n-1},A^{n-1},N^{n-1})}\otimes_{R}\mathbb{S}^{\nu_{[\ell]\backslash I}(\alpha^{n})}_{(M^{n},A^{n},N^{n})},

where
I
ranges over all subsets of
[\ell-1]
.

Proof.

Proceed by induction on
\ell
, where the base case
\ell=1
is trivial. Assume now that
\ell>1
and write each
\alpha^{i}=\beta^{i}\cdot\gamma^{i}
, where
\gamma^{i}:=p_{\ell}(\alpha^{i})
. By Lemma
5.15
there is a canonical short exact sequence

0\to\mathbb{S}^{\beta^{1}\cdot\gamma^{1},\dots,\beta^{n-1}\cdot\gamma^{n-1},\beta^{n}}_{\underline{M},\underline{A},\underline{N}}\otimes_{R}\mathbb{S}^{\gamma^{n}}_{M^{n},A^{n},N^{n}}

\to\mathbb{S}^{\beta^{1}\cdot\gamma^{1},\dots,\beta^{n}\cdot\gamma^{n}}_{\underline{M},\underline{A},\underline{N}}\to\mathbb{S}^{\beta^{1}\odot\gamma^{1},\dots,\beta^{n-1}\odot\gamma^{n-1},\beta^{n}\cdot\gamma^{n}}_{\underline{M},\underline{A},\underline{N}}\to 0.

By the inductive hypothesis, the multi-Schur module
\mathbb{S}^{\beta^{1}\cdot\gamma^{1},\dots,\beta^{n-1}\cdot\gamma^{n-1},\beta^{n}}_{\underline{M},\underline{A},\underline{N}}
admits a filtration with graded pieces of the form

\mathbb{S}^{\sigma_{I}(\alpha^{1}),\dots,\sigma_{I}(\alpha^{n-1})}_{(M^{1},A_{1},N^{1}),\dots,(M^{n-1},A^{n-1},N^{n-1})}\otimes_{R}\mathbb{S}^{\nu_{[\ell-1]\backslash I}(\beta^{n})}_{(M^{n},A^{n},N^{n})},

where
I\subset[\ell-2]
. Likewise, the multi-Schur module
\mathbb{S}^{\beta^{1}\odot\gamma^{1},\dots,\beta^{n-1}\odot\gamma^{n-1},\beta^{n}\cdot\gamma^{n}}_{\underline{M},\underline{A},\underline{N}}
admits a filtration with graded pieces of the form

\mathbb{S}^{\sigma_{J}(\beta^{1}\odot\gamma^{1}),\dots,\sigma_{J}(\beta^{n-1}\odot\gamma^{n-1})}_{(M^{1},A_{1},N^{1}),\dots,(M^{n-1},A^{n-1},N^{n-1})}\otimes_{R}\mathbb{S}^{\nu_{[\ell-1]\backslash J}(\beta^{n}\cdot\gamma^{n})}_{(M^{n},A^{n},N^{n})},

where
J\subset[\ell-2]
. Note that ranging over all
I\subset[\ell-2]
as in the first case is the same as ranging over all
I\subset[\ell-1]
with
\ell-1\notin I
, and ranging over all
J
as in the second case is equivalent to ranging over all
J\subset[\ell-1]
with
\ell-1\in J
. Combining both of these filtrations with Corollary
5.14
yields the result.
∎

Lemma 5.18
.

Let
\alpha
be any
\ell
-partitioned composition. Let
A
be a Koszul
R
-algebra and
N
(resp.
N
) any left (resp. right) Koszul
A
-module. For any
I\subset[\ell-1]
, the module
\mathbb{S}^{\nu_{I}(\alpha)}_{M,A,N}
admits a canonical filtration with associated graded pieces of the form

\mathbb{S}^{\sigma_{J}(\alpha)}_{M,A,N},

where
J\subset[\ell-1]
ranges over all subsets with
J\subseteq I
.

Proof.

Proceed by induction on
|I|
, with base case
I=\varnothing
being vacuous since there is an equality
\mathbb{S}^{\nu_{\varnothing}(\alpha)}_{M,A,N}=\mathbb{S}^{\sigma_{\varnothing}(\alpha)}_{M,A,N}
. For
|I|>0
, let
j\in I
be the largest element of
I
. Write
\alpha=p_{<j}(\alpha)\cdot p_{\geqslant j}(\alpha)
and consider the short exact sequence

0\to\mathbb{S}^{\nu_{I\backslash j}(\alpha)}_{M,A,N}\to\mathbb{S}^{\nu_{I}(\alpha)}_{M,A,N}\to\mathbb{S}^{\nu_{I\backslash j}(p_{<j}(\alpha)\odot p_{\geqslant j}(\alpha))}_{M,A,N}\to 0.

Let us consider the filtrations of the outer two terms: by the inductive hypothesis,
\mathbb{S}^{\nu_{I\backslash j}(\alpha)}_{M,A,N}
has a filtration with graded pieces of the form

\mathbb{S}^{\sigma_{K}(\alpha)}_{A},

where
K\subset[\ell-1]
ranges over all subsets with
K\subset I\backslash j
. Likewise, the term
\mathbb{S}^{\nu_{I\backslash j}(p_{<j}(\alpha)\odot p_{\geqslant j}(\alpha))}_{M,A,N}
has a filtration with graded pieces of the form

\mathbb{S}^{\sigma_{L}(p_{<j}(\alpha)\odot p_{\geqslant j}(\alpha))}_{M,A,N},

where
L\subset[\ell-2]
ranges over all subsets
L\subset s_{j}(I\backslash j)
. Notice that
p_{<j}(\alpha)\odot p_{\geqslant j}(\alpha))
is the same as
\sigma_{j}(\alpha)
, in which case ranging over all
K
and
L
as above is the same as just ranging over all subsets
J\subset[\ell-1]
with
J\subset I
. The result thus follows from Corollary
5.14
.
∎

Example 5.19
.

Let
A=S(V)
, the symmetric algebra on some free
R
-module
V
. Then
A
is a
\operatorname{{GL}}(V)
-representation and Lemma
5.18
implies that there is a
\operatorname{{GL}}(V)
-equivariant filtration of the tensor power
V^{\otimes d}
with graded pieces of the form
\mathbb{S}^{\alpha}_{A}
, where
\alpha
ranges over all compositions of
d
(since
V^{\otimes d}=\mathbb{S}^{\nu_{[d-1]}(1^{d})}_{S(V)}
).

Assuming
R
is a field of characteristic
0
, the ring
R[\operatorname{{GL}}(V)]
is semisimple and hence this yields a
\operatorname{{GL}}(V)
-equivariant direct sum decompositions

V^{\otimes d}=\bigoplus_{|\alpha|=d}\mathbb{S}^{\alpha}_{A}.

Finally, we arrive at the main result of this section; this result furnishes the multi-Schur functors with a canonical filtration whose associated graded pieces are easily described as tensor products of the ribbon Schur functors of Section
4
.

Theorem 5.20
.

Let
\alpha^{1},\dots,\alpha^{n}
be a sequence of
\ell
-partitioned compositions and
A^{1},\dots,A^{n}
be a sequence of Koszul
R
-algebras and
N^{i}
(resp.
M^{i}
) be Koszul left (resp. right)
A^{i}
-modules for
1\leqslant i\leqslant n
. Then the multi-Schur module
\mathbb{S}^{\underline{\alpha}}_{\underline{M},\underline{A},\underline{N}}
admits a canonical filtration with graded pieces of the form

\mathbb{S}^{\sigma_{I_{1}}(\alpha^{1})}_{(M^{1},A^{1},N^{1})}\otimes_{R}\mathbb{S}^{\sigma_{I_{2}}(\alpha^{2})}_{(M^{2},A^{2},N^{2})}\otimes_{R}\cdots\otimes_{R}\mathbb{S}^{\sigma_{I_{n}}(\alpha^{n})}_{(M^{n},A^{n},N^{n})},

where the subsets
I_{1},\dots,I_{n}\subset[\ell-1]
range over all choices such that
I_{1}\cap I_{2}\cap\cdots\cap I_{n}=\varnothing
.

Remark 5.21
.

Notice that the parameterizing set for the associated graded pieces is inherently symmetric under permuting the tensor factors, which is expected by the invariance of the multi-Schur module on the ordering of the inputs.

In general, the number of ways to choose
n
subsets of
[\ell-1]
with no common intersection is
(2^{n}-1)^{\ell-1}
, so the number of associated graded pieces grows exponentially with respect to both
n
and
\ell
.

Remark 5.22
.

The adjective “canonical” used in Theorem
5.20
means that this filtration is really true at the level of functors; in other words, the functor
\mathbb{S}^{\underline{\alpha}}
which takes as inputs
n
-tuples of Koszul
R
-algebras and outputs the associated multi-Schur module admits a canonical filtration whose associated graded pieces are given by the functors of the form

\mathbb{S}^{\sigma_{I_{1}}(\alpha^{1})}\otimes_{R}\mathbb{S}^{\sigma_{I_{2}}(\alpha^{2})}\otimes_{R}\cdots\otimes_{R}\mathbb{S}^{\sigma_{I_{n}}(\alpha^{n})}.

Proof of Theorem
5.20
.

Proceed by induction on
n
(the number of Koszul algebras), with base case
n=2
. To prove the base case, we induct on
\ell
(where the case
\ell=1
is vacuous). Let
\ell>1
and write
\alpha=\alpha^{\prime}\cdot p_{\ell}(\alpha)
and
\beta=\beta^{\prime}\cdot p_{\ell}(\beta)
. By Lemma
5.15
there is a short exact sequence

0\to\mathbb{S}^{\alpha,\beta^{\prime}}_{A,B}\otimes_{R}\mathbb{S}^{p_{\ell}(\beta)}_{B}\to\mathbb{S}^{\alpha,\beta}_{A,B}\to\mathbb{S}^{\alpha^{\prime}\odot p_{\ell}(\alpha),\beta}_{A,B}\to 0.

By the inductive hypothesis, the outer
2
terms admit filtrations with graded pieces of the correct form. This establishes the base case.

Assume now that
n>2
. By Lemma
5.17
the multi-Schur module
\mathbb{S}^{\alpha^{1},\dots,\alpha^{n}}_{A^{1},\dots,A^{n}}
admits a canonical filtration with associated graded pieces of the form

\mathbb{S}^{\sigma_{I}(\alpha^{1}),\dots,\sigma_{I}(\alpha^{n-1})}_{A^{1},\dots,A^{n-1}}\otimes_{R}\mathbb{S}^{\nu_{[\ell]\backslash I}(\alpha^{n})}_{A^{n}},

where
I
ranges over all subsets of
[\ell-1]
. By the inductive hypothesis, each of the modules
\mathbb{S}^{\sigma_{I}(\alpha^{1}),\dots,\sigma_{I}(\alpha^{n-1})}_{A^{1},\dots,A^{n-1}}
admits a canonical filtration with associated graded pieces of the form

\mathbb{S}^{\sigma_{I_{1}}(\alpha^{1})}_{A^{1}}\otimes_{R}\mathbb{S}^{\sigma_{I_{2}}(\alpha^{2})}_{A^{2}}\otimes_{R}\cdots\otimes_{R}\mathbb{S}^{\sigma_{I_{n-1}}(\alpha^{n-1})}_{A^{n-1}},

where the above ranges over all choices of
I_{1},\dots,I_{n-1}
with
I_{1}\cap\cdots\cap I_{n-1}=I
. On the other hand, the module
\mathbb{S}^{\nu_{[\ell-1]\backslash I}(\alpha^{n})}_{A^{n}}
admits a canonical filtration with associated graded pieces of the form
\mathbb{S}^{\sigma_{I_{n}}(\alpha^{n})}_{A^{n}}
, where
I_{n}
ranges over all subsets
I_{n}\subset[\ell-1]
such that
I_{n}\cap I=\varnothing
. This is overall the same thing as ranging over all choices
I_{1},\dots,I_{n}\subset[\ell-1]
such that
I_{1}\cap\cdots\cap I_{n}=\varnothing
.
∎

Example 5.23
.

Let
A
and
B
be Koszul algebras. Let us use the argument of Theorem
5.20
to filter the multi-Schur module
\mathbb{S}^{(1^{3}),(1^{3})}_{A,B}
, helping to illustrate the idea of the proof in a concrete setting. Recall by Example
5.16
that we have the short exact sequence

\mathbb{S}^{(1^{3}),(1^{2})}_{A,B}\otimes_{R}B_{1}\to\mathbb{S}^{(1^{3}),(1^{3})}_{A,B}\to\mathbb{S}^{(1,2),(1^{3})}_{A,B}\to 0.

Iteratively applying this sequence to the outer two terms yields

0\to A_{3}\otimes_{R}(B_{1})^{\otimes 3}\to\mathbb{S}^{(1^{3}),(1^{2})}_{A,B}\to\mathbb{S}^{(2,1)}_{A}\otimes_{R}\mathbb{S}^{(1^{2})}_{B}\otimes_{R}B_{1}\to 0,

0\to\mathbb{S}^{(1,2)}_{A}\otimes_{R}B_{1}\otimes_{R}\mathbb{S}^{(1^{2})}_{A}\to\mathbb{S}^{(1,2),(1^{3})}_{A,B}\to A_{3}\otimes_{R}\mathbb{S}^{(1^{3})}_{B}\to 0.

The terms involving
B
appearing on the ends of these short exact sequences are precisely the filtration factors of Lemma
5.17
, and are filtered further by Lemma
5.18
, yielding the filtration factors of Theorem
5.20
.

Remark 5.24
.

Let
2^{[\ell-1]}
denote the Boolean poset on
n
elements. Then the product
(2^{[\ell-1]})^{\times n}
is naturally a ranked poset (in fact, isomorphic to the Boolean poset
2^{[\ell-1]\times[n]}
), and the set
\mathcal{S}
of all tuples
(I_{1},\dots,I_{n})
with
I_{1}\cap\cdots\cap I_{n}=\varnothing
is a ranked subposet. Choosing any total order
<
refining the partial order on this subposet, the filtration of the multi-Schur module
\mathbb{S}^{\alpha^{1},\dots,\alpha^{n}}_{A^{1},\dots,A^{n}}
is parameterized by
<
. In other words, the filtration is of the form

\{F_{(I_{1},\dots,I_{n})}\}_{(I_{1},\dots,I_{n})\in\mathcal{S}},

with

F_{(I_{1},\dots,I_{n})}/F_{\text{pred}(I_{1},\dots,I_{n})}\cong\mathbb{S}^{\sigma_{I_{1}}(\alpha^{1})}_{A^{1}}\otimes_{R}\cdots\otimes_{R}\mathbb{S}^{\sigma_{I_{n}}(\alpha^{n})}_{A^{n}}.

In the above,
pred
denotes the predecessor function (ie, the largest element strictly smaller with respect to
<
).

Example 5.25
.

Let
\alpha=(1,2)\cdot(2)\cdot(1)
and
\beta=(3)\cdot(4,2)\cdot(2)
be two
3
-partitioned compositions and let us compute the composition factors of the multi-Schur module
\mathbb{S}^{\alpha,\beta}_{A,B}
for any two Koszul algebras
A
and
B
. The subposet (in fact, meet semilattice) of
(2^{[2]})^{\times 2}
that parametrizes the filtration factors has Hasse diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(\{1,2\},\varnothing)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(\{1\},\{2\})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(\{2\},\{1\})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(\varnothing,\{1,2\})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(\{1\},\varnothing)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(\{2\},\varnothing)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(\varnothing,\{1\})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(\varnothing,\{2\})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(\varnothing,\varnothing)}}

This translates to filtration factors of the following form:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{S}^{(1,5)}_{A}\otimes_{R}\mathbb{S}^{(3,4,2,2)}_{B}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{S}^{(1,4,1)}_{A}\otimes_{R}\mathbb{S}^{(3,4,4)}_{B}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{S}^{(1,2,3)}_{A}\otimes_{R}\mathbb{S}^{(7,2,2)}_{B}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{S}^{(1,2,2,1)}_{A}\otimes_{R}\mathbb{S}^{(7,4)}_{B}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{S}^{(1,4,1)}_{A}\otimes_{R}\mathbb{S}^{(3,4,2,2)}_{B}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{S}^{(1,2,3)}_{A}\otimes_{R}\mathbb{S}^{(3,4,2,2)}_{B}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{S}^{(1,2,2,1)}_{A}\otimes_{R}\mathbb{S}^{(7,2,2)}_{B}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{S}^{(1,2,2,1)}_{A}\otimes_{R}\mathbb{S}^{(3,4,4)}_{B}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{S}^{(1,2,2,1)}_{A}\otimes_{R}\mathbb{S}^{(3,4,2,2)}_{B}}}

If for instance
A=B=S(V)
, the symmetric algebra, then the above filtration is also
\operatorname{{GL}}(V)\times\operatorname{{GL}}(V)
equivariant (and in characteristic
0
yields a direct sum decomposition).

6.
Applications

The following section is the reward for enduring the technical details of Sections
4
and
5
; we are able to arrive at the other end with a rather robust theory that allows us to give elegant and simple closed form descriptions of higher derived invariants associated to (Veronese/Segre subalgebras of) Koszul algebras (see also
[
BM81
]
). In subsection
6.4
we show how to use this theory to build a large class of Koszul modules over an arbitrary Koszul algebra
A
, and specialize to the case of a polynomial ring to prove a uniform, characteristic-free regularity result for certain classes of vector bundles on projective space.

6.1.
Tor and Ext

In this section we prove the following theorem, which gives some concise descriptions of Tor and Ext between pairs of Koszul modules in terms of ribbon Schur functors.

Theorem 6.1
.

Let
A
be a Koszul algebra and
M
(resp.
N
) a Koszul right (resp. left)
A
-module of initial degree
t
(resp.
s
). Then there is a canonical isomorphism of
A
-modules

\operatorname{Tor}_{i}^{A}(M,N)\cong\mathbb{S}^{(1^{i})}_{M,A,N}\quad\text{for all}\ i>0

If
M
is instead a Koszul left
A
-module, then there is a canonical isomorphism

\operatorname{Ext}^{i}_{A}(M,N)\cong\mathbb{S}^{(i)}_{M^{!},A^{!},(N^{*})^{!}}\quad\text{for all}\ i>0.

In particular, both of the above modules are flat
R
-modules annihilated by
A_{+}
.

Proof.

By the definition of Tor combined with Theorem
A.25
, the module
\operatorname{Tor}_{i}^{A}(M,N)
may be computed by looking at the homology of the complex

\cdots\to M\otimes_{R}\mathbb{S}^{(1^{i+1})}_{A,N}\to M\otimes_{R}\mathbb{S}^{(1^{i})}_{A,N}\to M\otimes_{R}\mathbb{S}^{(1^{i-1})}_{A,N}\to\cdots.

Splitting this complex into graded pieces, there is a commutative diagram:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{M_{j}\otimes_{R}\mathbb{S}^{(1^{i})}_{A,N}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{M_{j+1}\otimes_{R}\mathbb{S}^{(1^{i-1})}_{A,N}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{M_{j}\otimes_{R}A_{1}\otimes_{R}\mathbb{S}^{(1^{i-1})}_{A.N}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{S}^{(1)}_{M_{\geqslant j},A}\otimes_{R}\mathbb{S}^{(1^{i-1})}_{A,N}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(d_{i})_{j+i+s}}

This implies that there is an equality

\operatorname{Ker}(d_{i})_{i+j+s}=\left(M_{j}\otimes_{R}\mathbb{S}^{(1^{i})}_{A,N}\right)\cap\left(\mathbb{S}^{(1)}_{M_{\geqslant j},A}\otimes_{R}\mathbb{S}^{(1^{i-1})}_{A,N}\right).

By Observation
4.41
, this latter intersection is precisely
\mathbb{S}^{(1^{i})}_{(M_{\geqslant j},A,N)}
. On the other hand, by definition of the Priddy differential there is also a commutative diagram for all
j>s
:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{M_{j-1}\otimes_{R}\mathbb{S}^{(1^{i+1})}_{A,N}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{M_{j}\otimes_{R}\mathbb{S}^{(1^{i})}_{A,N}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{S}^{(1^{i})}_{M_{\geqslant j},A,N}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{M_{j}\otimes_{R}A_{1}\otimes_{R}\mathbb{S}^{(1^{i-1})}_{A,N}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(d_{i+1})_{j+i+s}}

This implies that there is also an equality

\operatorname{im}(d_{i+1})_{j+i+s}=\mathbb{S}^{(1^{i})}_{M_{\geqslant j},A,N}.

Putting both of the above equalities together, it follows that

\operatorname{Tor}_{i}^{A}(M,N)_{i+j+s}=\begin{cases}\mathbb{S}^{(1^{i})}_{M,A,N}&\text{if}\ j=s,\\
0&\text{otherwise}.\end{cases}

To prove the isomorphism for Ext, recall first that there is a canonical isomorphism

\operatorname{Ext}^{i}_{A}(M,N)\cong\left(\operatorname{Tor}_{i}^{A}(N^{*},M)\right)^{*}.

By Observation
A.22
, the graded dual
N^{*}
is a Koszul right
A
-module, and by the isomorphism just proved for Tor there is an isomorphism

\operatorname{Tor}_{i}^{A}(N^{*},M)\cong\mathbb{S}^{(1^{i})}_{N^{*},A,M}.

Dualizing and using the isomorphism of Corollary
4.45
(3), the result follows immediately.
∎

Example 6.2
.

Assume that
A
is any Koszul algebra and recall that

\mathbb{S}_{A,A_{+}^{d}}^{(1^{i})}=\mathbb{S}^{(1^{i},d)}_{A}.

By Theorem
A.25
, the minimal free resolution of
A_{+}^{d}
thus has the form

\cdots\to A\otimes_{R}\mathbb{S}^{(1^{i},d)}_{A}\to A\otimes_{R}\mathbb{S}^{(1^{i-1},d)}_{A}\to\cdots\to A\otimes_{R}A_{d}\to A_{+}^{d}.

The ribbon Schur module
\mathbb{S}^{(1^{i},d)}_{A}
may be presented as the cokernel of the composition

(A^{!})^{*}_{i+2}\otimes_{R}A_{d-2}\to(A^{!})^{*}_{i+1}\otimes_{R}A_{1}\otimes_{R}A_{d-2}\to(A^{!})^{*}_{i+1}\otimes_{R}A_{d-1},

in which case we see that there is an isomorphism with the ribbon Schur functor:

\mathbb{S}^{(1^{i},d)}_{A}\cong L^{A}_{i-1,d-1},

where the module
L^{A}_{i+1,d-1}
is defined as in
[
FJKL+20
]
. Thus Theorem
6.1
at least recovers the minimal free resolution of powers of the maximal ideal of a Koszul algebra constructed in
[
FJKL+20
]
.

Corollary 6.3
.

Let
A
be any Koszul algebra such that
\mathbb{S}^{(1^{i})}_{A}=0
for all
i>1
. Then every Koszul module over
A
is a flat
R
-module. If
A
is
R
-projective, then every Koszul module over
A
is
R
-projective.

6.2.
Veronese Subalgebras

Recall the definition of the Veronese subalgebra as in Definition
2.4
. The following observation shows that the operation
(-)^{(d)}
on compositions as defined in Definition
4.1
interacts functorially with the formation of the ribbon Schur functor:

Observation 6.4
.

Let
A
be any Koszul algebra and
M
(resp.
N
) any Koszul right (resp. left)
A
-module. For any integer
d>0
and integers
i,j\in\mathbb{Z}
there are isomorphisms

\mathbb{S}^{\alpha}_{{A^{(d)}}}=\mathbb{S}^{\alpha^{(d)}}_{A},\quad\text{and}\quad\mathbb{S}^{\alpha}_{{{M}^{(d)}},{A^{(d)}},{{N}^{(d)}}}\cong\mathbb{S}^{\alpha^{(d)}}_{M,A,N}.

Combining Theorem
6.1
with Observation
6.4
immediately yields:

Corollary 6.5
.

Let
A
be any Koszul algebra and
M
(resp.
N
) any Koszul right (resp. left)
A
-module. For any integer
d>0
there is an isomorphism

\operatorname{Tor}_{i}^{{A^{(d)}}}({{M}^{(d)}},{{N}^{(d)}})=\mathbb{S}^{(d^{i})}_{M,A,N},

where
t
(resp.
s
) is the initial degree of
M
(resp.
N
). In particular, there are canonical isomorphisms

\operatorname{Tor}_{i}^{{A^{(d)}}}({A^{({\geqslant r},{d})}},{A^{({\geqslant r^{\prime}},{d})}})\cong\mathbb{S}^{(r,d^{i},r^{\prime})}_{A}.

Remark 6.6
.

The isomorphism

\operatorname{Tor}_{i}^{{A^{(d)}}}({A^{({\geqslant r},{d})}},{A^{({\geqslant r^{\prime}},{d})}})\cong\mathbb{S}^{(r,d^{i},r^{\prime})}_{A}.

was originally proved in the case
A=S(V)
(the symmetric algebra) in the work
[
APP+22
]
. However, one notices that the original proof of this isomorphism does not invoke anything more than the Koszulness properties of the symmetric algebra (and its truncations), which leads to the generalization presented in Corollary
6.5
.

6.3.
Segre Subalgebras

In this subsection, we apply the construction of multi-Schur modules and their filtrations to study Segre products of Koszul algebras. The following observation is a straightforward translation of the quadratic dual for a Koszul
R
-algebra:

Observation 6.7
.

Let
A^{1},\dots,A^{n}
be a collection of Koszul algebras and
M^{i}
a Koszul left
A^{i}
-module for
1\leqslant i\leqslant n
. Then there are isomorphisms of
R
-modules

\left((A^{1}\circ\cdots\circ A^{n})^{!}\right)^{*}_{i}=\mathbb{S}^{(1^{i})}_{A^{1},\dots,A^{n}},\quad\text{and}

\left((M^{1}\circ\cdots\circ M^{n})^{!}\right)^{*}_{i}=\mathbb{S}^{(1^{i})}_{(A^{1},M^{1}),\dots,(A^{n},M^{n})}.

Example 6.8
.

Let
A
be any Koszul algebra and consider the filtration of Theorem
5.20
applied to computing
(A^{[2]\ !})^{*}_{3}=\mathbb{S}^{(1^{3})}_{A,A}
. The poset parametrizing the filtration terms are the same as those of Example
5.25
, which yields the filtration factors

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{S}^{(3)}_{A}\otimes_{R}\mathbb{S}^{(1^{3})}_{A}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{S}^{(2,1)}_{A}\otimes_{R}\mathbb{S}^{(1,2)}_{A}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{S}^{(1,2)}_{A}\otimes_{R}\mathbb{S}^{(2,1)}_{A}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{S}^{(1^{3})}_{A}\otimes_{R}\mathbb{S}^{(3)}_{A}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{S}^{(2,1)}_{A}\otimes_{R}\mathbb{S}^{(1^{3})}_{A}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{S}^{(1,2)}_{A}\otimes_{R}\mathbb{S}^{(1^{3})}_{A}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{S}^{(1^{3})}_{A}\otimes_{R}\mathbb{S}^{(2,1)}_{A}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{S}^{(1^{3})}_{A}\otimes_{R}\mathbb{S}^{(1,2)}_{A}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{S}^{(1^{3})}_{A}\otimes_{R}\mathbb{S}^{(1^{3})}_{A}}}

Suppose now that
A=S(V)
, the symmetric algebra on a vector space
V
. If
R
is a field of characteristic
0
, this induces a
\operatorname{{GL}}(V)\times\operatorname{{GL}}(V)
-equivariant direct sum decomposition of
(A^{[2]\ !})^{*}_{3}
into irreducibles:

(A^{[2]\ !})^{*}_{3}=\left(\bigwedge^{3}V\otimes\bigwedge^{3}V\right)\oplus\left(\bigwedge^{3}V\otimes_{R}\mathbb{S}^{(2,1)}(V)\right)^{\oplus 4}

\oplus\left(\bigwedge^{3}V\otimes S_{3}(V)\right)^{\oplus 2}\oplus\left(\mathbb{S}^{(2,1)}(V)\otimes\mathbb{S}^{(2,1)}(V)\right)^{\oplus 2}.

Observation 6.9
.

Let
\alpha\in C(d)
be any composition of some integer
d>0
and
A^{1},\dots,A^{n}
a sequence of Koszul
R
-algebras admitting a compatible
G^{i}
-action for each
1\leqslant i\leqslant n
. Assume that
M^{i}
(resp.
N^{i}
) is a Koszul right (resp. left)
A^{i}
-module admitting a compatible
G^{i}
-action. Then the multi-Schur module

\mathbb{S}^{\alpha}_{(M^{1},A^{1},N^{1}),\dots,(M^{n},A^{n},N^{n})}

admits a
G^{1}\times\cdots\times G^{n}
-equivariant filtration with associated graded pieces of the form

\mathbb{S}^{\alpha^{1}}_{(M^{1},A^{1},N^{1})}\otimes_{R}\cdots\otimes_{R}\mathbb{S}^{\alpha^{n}}_{(M^{n},A^{n},N^{n})},

where
\alpha^{1},\dots,\alpha^{n}
range over all compositions of
|\alpha|
satisfying
\alpha^{1}\wedge\cdots\wedge\alpha^{n}=\alpha
.

In particular, if each of the group rings
R[G^{i}]
is semisimple, then there is a
G^{1}\times\cdots\times G^{n}
-equivariant decomposition

\mathbb{S}^{\alpha}_{(M^{1},A^{1},N^{1}),\dots,(M^{n},A^{n},N^{n})}\cong\bigoplus_{\begin{subarray}{c}(\alpha^{1},\dots,\alpha^{n})\in C(d)^{\times n}\\
\alpha^{1}\wedge\cdots\wedge\alpha^{n}=\alpha\end{subarray}}\mathbb{S}^{\alpha^{1}}_{(M^{1},A^{1},N^{1})}\otimes_{R}\cdots\otimes_{R}\mathbb{S}^{\alpha^{n}}_{(M^{n},A^{n},N^{n})}.

Proof.

This is just a retranslation of Theorem
5.20
combined with the fact that the refinement poset on
\alpha
is isomorphic to the Boolean poset on
[\ell(\alpha)-1]
, and the meet operation corresponds to intersection under this isomorphism.
∎

Remark 6.10
.

The appearance of compositions ranging over the refinement poset as filtration factors is likely related to the connection between Segre products and internal cohomomorphisms as discovered by Manin (see
[
Man87
]
and
[
Man14
]
). Indeed, another perspective on the filtration
5.20
is as a canonical filtration of the graded pieces of the cohomomorphism algebra.

Next, we use Theorem
5.20
to prove a symmetric function identity; we first need to recall some notation related to Schur polynomials and establish some multi-index conventions:

Notation 6.11
.

Let
n\geqslant 1
be any integer and consider sets of indeterminates
\mathbf{x}^{1},\dots,\mathbf{x}^{n}
. Recall that the
Schur polynomial
associated to a skew shape
\lambda/\mu
is the polynomial

s_{\lambda/\mu}(\mathbf{x}):=\sum_{T\in\operatorname{SST}(\lambda/\mu)}\mathbf{x}_{T},

where
\mathbf{x}_{T}
denotes the multigraded character of the semistandard tableau
T
. If
\alpha
is a composition, the notation
s_{\alpha}(\mathbf{x})
denotes the Schur polynomial corresponding to the ribbon shape determined by
\alpha
. The complete symmetric polynomial
h_{d}(\mathbf{x})
is defined to be
s_{(d)}(\mathbf{x})
.

Given a tuple of compositions
\underline{\alpha}=(\alpha^{1},\dots,\alpha^{n})
, use the notation

s_{\underline{\alpha}}(\underline{\mathbf{x}}):=s_{\alpha^{1}}(\mathbf{x}^{1})\cdot s_{\alpha^{2}}(\mathbf{x}^{2})\cdots s_{\alpha^{n}}(\mathbf{x}^{n}).

In particular, for a single composition
\alpha=(\alpha_{1},\dots,\alpha_{n})
there is the equality

h_{\alpha}(\underline{\mathbf{x}})=h_{\alpha_{1}}(\mathbf{x}^{1})\cdot h_{\alpha_{2}}(\mathbf{x}^{2})\cdots h_{\alpha_{n}}(\mathbf{x}^{n}).

Corollary 6.12
.

With notation as in Notation
6.11
, there is an equality of symmetric polynomials

h_{d^{n}+\alpha}(\underline{\mathbf{x}})=\sum_{i=1}^{d}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\beta^{1},\dots,\beta^{n}\\
|\beta^{1}|=\cdots=|\beta^{n}|=i\\
\beta^{1}\wedge\cdots\wedge\beta^{n}=1^{i}\end{subarray}}(-1)^{i+1}h_{(d-i)^{n}}(\underline{\mathbf{x}})\cdot s_{\underline{\beta}\cdot\alpha}(\underline{\mathbf{x}}).

Remark 6.13
.

In the statement of Corollary
6.12
, if
\underline{\beta}=(\beta^{1},\dots,\beta^{n})
is a tuple of compositions and
\alpha=(\alpha_{1},\dots,\alpha_{n})
is a composition, then we use the convention

\underline{\beta}\cdot\alpha:=\left(\beta^{1}\cdot(\alpha_{1}),\beta^{2}\cdot(\alpha_{2}),\dots,\beta^{n}\cdot(\alpha_{n})\right).

Proof.

Assume
R=k
is a field of characteristic
0
and let
A=S(V)
for some vector space
V
. Note that
A
and
A_{\geqslant d}=A_{+}^{d}
are polynomial functors for all
d\geqslant 1
. Taking Segre products, this means that
A^{[n]}
and
A_{+}^{\alpha}:=A_{+}^{\alpha_{1}}\circ A_{+}^{\alpha_{2}}\circ\cdots\circ A_{+}^{\alpha_{n}}
are also polynomial functors, and hence there is an equality.

\operatorname{Ch}(A_{+}^{\alpha})=\operatorname{Ch}(A^{[n]})\cdot\sum_{i\geqslant 0}(-1)^{i}\operatorname{Ch}\left(\operatorname{Tor}_{i}^{A^{[n]}}(A_{+}^{\alpha},k)\right),

(6.1)

where
\operatorname{Ch}(-)
denotes the multigraded character. By definition there are equalities

\operatorname{Ch}(A_{+}^{\alpha})=\sum_{d\geqslant 1}h_{d^{n}+\alpha}(\underline{\mathbf{x}}),

\operatorname{Ch}(A^{[n]})=\sum_{d\geqslant 0}h_{d^{n}}(\underline{\mathbf{x}}).

On the other hand, by Theorem
5.20
there is an equality

\operatorname{Ch}\left(\operatorname{Tor}_{i}^{A^{[n]}}(A_{+}^{\alpha},k)\right)=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\beta^{1},\dots,\beta^{n}\\
|\beta^{1}|=\cdots=|\beta^{n}|=i\\
\beta^{1}\wedge\cdots\wedge\beta^{n}=1^{i}\end{subarray}}s_{\underline{\beta}\cdot\alpha}(\underline{\mathbf{x}}).

Combining all of these expressions and comparing degrees on each side of the equality (
6.1
) yields the result.
∎

Remark 6.14
.

When
n=1
, this reduces to the well-known classical character identity

h_{d+a}(\mathbf{x})=\sum_{i=1}^{d}(-1)^{i+1}h_{d-i}(\mathbf{x})s_{(1^{i},a)}(\mathbf{x}).

Moreover, taking
s
th Veronese powers of the polynomial ring and performing an identical argument yields the same identity, but every composition is replaced with its
s
th rescaling (ie, apply the operation
(-)^{(s)}
to all compositions).

Example 6.15
.

Let
n=2
,
d=2
, and
\alpha=(\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2})
be any composition. Then the identity of Corollary
6.12
reads

h_{2+\alpha_{1}}(\mathbf{x}^{1})h_{2+\alpha_{2}}(\mathbf{x}^{2})=h_{d-1}(\mathbf{x}^{1})h_{d-1}(\mathbf{x}^{2})s_{(1,\alpha_{1})}(\mathbf{x}^{1})s_{(1,\alpha_{2})}(\mathbf{x}^{2})

-s_{(1^{2},\alpha_{1})}(\mathbf{x}^{1})s_{(1^{2},\alpha_{2})}(\mathbf{x}^{2})-s_{(1^{2},\alpha_{1})}(\mathbf{x}^{1})s_{(2,\alpha_{2})}(\mathbf{x}^{2})-s_{(2,\alpha_{1})}(\mathbf{x}^{1})s_{(1^{2},\alpha_{2})}(\mathbf{x}^{2}).

Our next corollary is an evident consequence of Theorem
6.1
combined with Observation
6.9
(by setting
\alpha=(1^{i})
).

Corollary 6.16
.

Let
A^{1},\dots,A^{n}
a sequence of Koszul
R
-algebras admitting a compatible
G^{i}
-action for each
1\leqslant i\leqslant n
. Assume that
M^{i}
(resp.
N^{i}
) is a Koszul right (resp. left)
A^{i}
-module admitting a compatible
G^{i}
-action. Then the module

\operatorname{Tor}_{i}^{A^{1}\circ\cdots\circ A^{n}}(M^{1}\circ\cdots\circ M^{n},N^{1}\circ\cdots\circ N^{n})

admits a
G^{1}\times\cdots\times G^{n}
-equivariant filtration with associated graded pieces of the form

\mathbb{S}^{\alpha^{1}}_{M^{1},A^{1},N^{1}}\otimes_{R}\cdots\otimes_{R}\mathbb{S}^{\alpha^{n}}_{M^{n},A^{n},N^{n}},

where the compositions range over all tuples
(\alpha^{1},\dots,\alpha^{n})\in C(i)^{\times n}
with
\alpha^{1}\wedge\cdots\wedge\alpha^{n}=(1^{i})
.

Likewise, the module

\operatorname{Tor}_{i}^{(A^{1}\circ\cdots\circ A^{n})^{(d)}}((M^{1}\circ\cdots\circ M^{n})^{(d)},(N^{1}\circ\cdots\circ N^{n})^{(d)})

admits a
G^{1}\times\cdots\times G^{n}
-equivariant filtration with associated graded pieces of the form

\mathbb{S}^{\alpha^{1}}_{M^{1},A^{1},N^{1}}\otimes_{R}\cdots\otimes_{R}\mathbb{S}^{\alpha^{n}}_{M^{n},A^{n},N^{n}},

where the compositions range over all tuples
(\alpha^{1},\dots,\alpha^{n})\in C(di)^{\times n}
with
\alpha^{1}\wedge\cdots\wedge\alpha^{n}=(d^{i})
.

Example 6.17
.

Let
A
be any Koszul algebra and let us compute the filtration factors of
(A^{[3]!})^{*}_{2}=\mathbb{S}^{(1^{2})}_{A^{[3]}}
(unfortunately, trying this for
\mathbb{S}^{(1^{3})}_{A^{[3]}}
yields
49
filtration factors, which is too big of an example). The poset parametrizing the filtration factors is:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(\{1\},\{1\},\varnothing)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(\{1\},\varnothing,\{1\})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(\varnothing,\{1\},\{1\})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(\{1\},\varnothing,\varnothing)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(\varnothing,\{1\},\varnothing)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(\varnothing,\varnothing,\{1\})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(\varnothing,\varnothing,\varnothing)}}

This yields filtration factors:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{S}^{(2)}_{A}\otimes_{R}\mathbb{S}^{(2)}_{A}\otimes_{R}\mathbb{S}^{(1^{2})}_{A}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{S}^{(2)}_{A}\otimes_{R}\mathbb{S}^{(1^{2})}_{A}\otimes_{R}\mathbb{S}^{(2)}_{A}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{S}^{(1^{2})}_{A}\otimes_{R}\mathbb{S}^{(2)}_{A}\otimes_{R}\mathbb{S}^{(2)}_{A}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{S}^{(2)}_{A}\otimes_{R}\mathbb{S}^{(1^{2})}_{A}\otimes_{R}\mathbb{S}^{(1^{2})}_{A}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{S}^{(1^{2})}_{A}\otimes_{R}\mathbb{S}^{(2)}_{A}\otimes_{R}\mathbb{S}^{(1^{2})}_{A}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{S}^{(1^{2})}_{A}\otimes_{R}\mathbb{S}^{(1^{2})}_{A}\otimes_{R}\mathbb{S}^{(2)}_{A}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{S}^{(1^{2})}_{A}\otimes_{R}\mathbb{S}^{(1^{2})}_{A}\otimes_{R}\mathbb{S}^{(1^{2})}_{A}}}

Example 6.18
.

Let
A
be any Koszul algebra and let
T(V)
denote the tensor algebra on some projective
R
-module
V
. Then by definition the
R
-module
\mathbb{S}^{(1^{i})}_{A,T(V)}
admits a filtration with graded pieces of the form

\mathbb{S}^{\alpha^{1}}_{A}\otimes_{R}\mathbb{S}^{\alpha^{2}}_{T(V)},

with
\alpha^{1}\wedge\alpha^{2}=(1^{i})
. Note that the only choice of
\alpha^{2}
for which
\mathbb{S}^{\alpha^{2}}_{T(V)}
is nonzero is for
\alpha^{2}=(i)
. Thus
\alpha^{1}=(1^{i})
and we find

\mathbb{S}^{(1^{i})}_{A,T(V)}=\mathbb{S}^{(1^{i})}_{A}\otimes_{R}\mathbb{S}^{(i)}_{T(V)}=(A^{!})^{*}_{i}\otimes_{R}V^{\otimes i}.

In particular, after dualizing and collecting graded pieces, there is an isomorphism of
R
-algebras

(A\circ T(V))^{!}=A^{!}\circ T(V^{*}).

Of course, this could be verified using more direct methods, but the point is to demonstrate the utility of Theorem
5.20
.

Example 6.19
.

Let
A=S(V)
,
B=S(W)
be symmetric algebras on free
R
-modules
V
and
W
both of rank
2
. Let
M=A_{+}^{r}\circ B
and
N=A\circ B_{+}^{r^{\prime}}
, viewed as modules over the Segre product
A\circ B
. By Corollary
6.16
there is an equality

\operatorname{Tor}^{A\circ B}_{i}(M,N)=\mathbb{S}^{(1^{i})}_{(A,A_{+}^{r})\circ(B,B_{+}^{r^{\prime}})}.

The module
\mathbb{S}^{(1^{i})}_{(A,A_{+}^{r})\circ(B,B_{+}^{r^{\prime}})}
has a
\operatorname{{GL}}(V)\times\operatorname{{GL}}(W)
-equivariant filtration with associated graded pieces of the form
